| Introduce experience and contribute in interviews/work | [ADD01](#add01), [ADD02](#add02), [P075](#existing-p075), [WORK02](#work02), [WORK18](#work18), [ADD17](#add17) | Introductions, contribution/results, follow-ups, requests with deadlines added. |
| Describe a technical problem, evidence, constraints, and trade-offs | [P059](#existing-p059), [P066](#existing-p066), [PROBLEM08](#problem08), [TECH11](#tech11), [TECH07](#tech07) | Preserve useful technical examples; route individual engineering verbs to vocabulary. |
| Report another speaker, describe people/things, and control reference | [ADD34](#add34), [P102](#existing-p102), [P103](#existing-p103), [ADD39](#add39), [ADD40](#add40), [ADD42](#add42) | Add practical reporting, existence, quantity, and article contrasts; reference existing relative-clause knowledge. |
| Describe symptoms or ask for urgent help | [ADD19](#add19) | Add communication examples, with duration grammar reused. |
| Sustain a story or explanation across sentences | [P063](#existing-p063), [P098](#existing-p098), [FLOW08](#flow08), [ADD48](#add48) | Practice connected speech, interruptions, and follow-up questions; not another list of linking words. |

## Consolidated entries

IDs such as `TIME03` refer to the original section and item number. The prefix matches the section title below; `ADD` identifies coverage introduced in this review. All original IDs occur once in **Sources**. P-numbers refer only to the [existing-card index](#existing-patterns-card-index), not to new Anki identifiers.

The status labels below describe the original catalog audit; **Replacement disposition** records the implemented result. Use these entries as a coverage reference, not a must-learn checklist. If the learner can already use a frame flexibly, keep it as a reference instead of adding reviews. Previously rejected vocabulary has not been reinstated as new vocabulary cards.

## Time and sequence

<a id="time01"></a>

### TIME01 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `I'd just + past participle + when + past clause`

**Use:** Say something happened immediately after you finished an action.

**Example:** I'd just sat down when my phone rang.

**Usage / decision:** Past perfect plus just/when is a narrative application of the existing earlier-past contrast.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P094](#existing-p094).

**Replacement disposition:** [N059](cards.md#simple-past--had--past-participle--had-been--v-ing)

**Sources:** TIME01.

<a id="time02"></a>

### TIME02 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I was about to + verb + when + past clause`

**Use:** Describe an action that was imminent when another event occurred.

**Example:** I was about to leave when the power went out.

**Usage / decision:** About to does not guarantee the intended action was prevented.

**Replacement disposition:** [N060](cards.md#be-about-to--verb--when--event--waswere-going-to--verb-but--clause--meant-to--verb-but--clause)

**Sources:** TIME02.

<a id="time03"></a>

### TIME03 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `As soon as / Once / The moment + clause, main clause`

**Use:** Link an action to the time or stage when another event happens.

**Example:** As soon as I get home, I'll call you. / Once we have approval, we can schedule the release. / The moment I saw the error, I stopped the deployment.

**Usage / decision:** As soon as and the moment emphasize immediate sequence; once emphasizes reaching the condition or stage. Once does not promise immediate action. Use a present form in future time clauses.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C05-044](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-044), [C05-046](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-046), [C05-047](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-047).

**Replacement disposition:** [N062](cards.md#as-soon-asoncethe-moment--clause--main-clause--right-after--nounclause)

**Sources:** TIME03, TIME05, TIME17.

<a id="time04"></a>

### TIME04 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `By the time + past clause, had + past participle`

**Use:** Say an event was already finished when another happened.

**Example:** By the time I arrived, everyone had left.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P097](#existing-p097).

**Replacement disposition:** [N064](cards.md#by-the-time--clause--perfect-form)

**Sources:** TIME04.

<a id="time06"></a>

### TIME06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `subject + was/were + V-ing + when + past clause`

**Use:** Set a past activity in progress as the background to another event.

**Example:** I was cooking when the fire alarm went off.

**Usage / decision:** One narrative construction; realizing something is one possible event, not a separate pattern.

**Replacement disposition:** [N058](cards.md#waswere--v-ing--when--past-event--while--past-continuous--past-continuous)

**Sources:** TIME06, STORY04.

<a id="time07"></a>

### TIME07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `While + past continuous, past continuous`

**Use:** Describe two actions happening simultaneously in the past.

**Example:** While I was driving, she was looking at the map.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C05-038](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-038).

**Replacement disposition:** [N058](cards.md#waswere--v-ing--when--past-event--while--past-continuous--past-continuous)

**Sources:** TIME07.

<a id="time08"></a>

### TIME08 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `have/has been + V-ing + for/since; How long have/has + subject + been + V-ing?`

**Use:** Describe how long an activity has continued until now.

**Example:** I've been studying English for six months.

**Usage / decision:** Duration, recent frequency, and the duration question belong to the existing continuing-activity family. Question inversion is a form change, not three new notes.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P089](#existing-p089).

**Replacement disposition:** [N054](cards.md#havehas--past-participle--havehas-been--v-ing--for--duration--since--starting-point)

**Sources:** TIME08, AMOUNT03, QUESTION04.

<a id="time09"></a>

### TIME09 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `I've + past participle + since + starting point`

**Use:** Say something started at a particular point and continues.

**Example:** I've lived here since 2022.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P091](#existing-p091).

**Replacement disposition:** [N052](cards.md#simple-past--havehas--past-participle--have-you-ever---havehas-not--yet)

**Sources:** TIME09.

<a id="time10"></a>

### TIME10 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Have you ever + past participle? / I've never + past participle + before`

**Use:** Say you've never experienced something before now.

**Example:** Have you ever worked abroad? / I've never worked abroad, but I've collaborated with international teams.

**Usage / decision:** Ask about experience up to now and give a negative answer. Ever is not required in affirmative experience statements. Add experience, long-gap, or already examples if these uses are not active; reuse the present-perfect family rather than notes for every adverb.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P090](#existing-p090).

**Replacement disposition:** [N052](cards.md#simple-past--havehas--past-participle--have-you-ever---havehas-not--yet)

**Sources:** TIME10, SOCIAL11.

<a id="time11"></a>

### TIME11 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `This is the first time I've + past participle`

**Use:** Say an experience is happening for the first time.

**Example:** This is the first time I've tried this restaurant.

**Usage / decision:** This is the first time I've done is a current first occasion; The first time I did locates a past event. Add the current-occasion form, preserving both meanings.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P096](#existing-p096).

**Replacement disposition:** [N055](cards.md#the-firstlast-time--past-clause--clause--this-is-the-first-time--present-perfect--when-was-the-last-time--past-clause)

**Sources:** TIME11.

<a id="time12"></a>

### TIME12 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `I haven't + past participle + in ages`

**Use:** Say you're doing something again after a long gap.

**Example:** I haven't seen him in ages.

**Usage / decision:** Add experience, long-gap, or already examples if these uses are not active; reuse the present-perfect family rather than notes for every adverb.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P090](#existing-p090).

**Replacement disposition:** [N052](cards.md#simple-past--havehas--past-participle--have-you-ever---havehas-not--yet)

**Sources:** TIME12.

<a id="time13"></a>

### TIME13 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `used to + base verb / be used to + noun or V-ing / get used to + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Say something was habitual but isn't anymore.

**Example:** I used to commute. / I'm used to working remotely. / I'm still getting used to the time difference.

**Usage / decision:** Used to describes a past habit or state; be used to describes familiarity; get used to describes developing familiarity. Keep the existing contrast and add the transition form. Add get used to; the other two forms already exist.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P035](#existing-p035).

**Replacement disposition:** [N057](cards.md#used-to--base-verb--would--base-verb-for-repeated-past-actions--beget-used-to--nounv-ing)

**Sources:** TIME13, TIME14, TIME15.

<a id="time16"></a>

### TIME16 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `I've just started + V-ing`

**Use:** Say you only recently began doing something.

**Example:** I've just started learning to cook.

**Usage / decision:** A recent-start example of start + V-ing/to-infinitive and present-perfect time. Keep for practice instead of a special just-started note.

**Original home / related coverage:** Catalog: [TIME10](#time10).

**Replacement disposition:** [N052](cards.md#simple-past--havehas--past-participle--have-you-ever---havehas-not--yet)

**Sources:** TIME16.

<a id="time18"></a>

### TIME18 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'll + verb + by + time`

**Use:** Promise or predict completion no later than a deadline.

**Example:** I'll send you the file by Friday.

**Replacement disposition:** [N063](cards.md#by--deadline--until--endpoint--not--until--timeclause--it-was-not-until-x-that-y)

**Sources:** TIME18.

<a id="time19"></a>

### TIME19 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Whenever + clause, clause`

**Use:** Say something happens every time a condition occurs.

**Example:** Whenever I drink coffee late, I can't sleep.

**Replacement disposition:** [N066](cards.md#wheneverevery-time--clause--clause--whenif--clause--clause)

**Sources:** TIME19.

<a id="time20"></a>

### TIME20 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Over time, + clause`

**Use:** Describe how your circumstances evolved over time.

**Example:** Over time, I've become more comfortable speaking English.

**Usage / decision:** Over time emphasizes gradual development and does not require the present perfect. Compare with over the past + period.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P081](#existing-p081).

**Replacement disposition:** [N067](cards.md#at-first--clause-eventuallyin-the-end--clause--over-time--clause--over-the-past--period--present-perfect)

**Sources:** TIME20.

<a id="time21"></a>

### TIME21 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `already + appropriate verb phrase`

**Use:** Say a state or event is true by now, often earlier than expected.

**Example:** It's only five, and the sun's already setting.

**Usage / decision:** Already works with several tenses: I already know; I already sent it; it's already getting dark. Add experience, long-gap, or already examples if these uses are not active; reuse the present-perfect family rather than notes for every adverb.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P090](#existing-p090).

**Replacement disposition:** [N052](cards.md#simple-past--havehas--past-participle--have-you-ever---havehas-not--yet)

**Sources:** TIME21.

<a id="time22"></a>

### TIME22 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `have/has not + past participle + yet / Have/Has + subject + past participle + yet?`

**Use:** Say an event hasn't occurred but is expected.

**Example:** I haven't heard back from them yet.

**Usage / decision:** The question and negative are variants of the same completion-status frame. Did you finish yet? is also normal casual AmE; do not teach the present perfect as the only permitted form.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P090](#existing-p090).

**Replacement disposition:** [N052](cards.md#simple-past--havehas--past-participle--have-you-ever---havehas-not--yet)

**Sources:** TIME22, QUESTION08.

<a id="time23"></a>

### TIME23 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `I + past verb + right away`

**Use:** State you did something immediately without delay.

**Example:** I called her right away.

**Usage / decision:** Existing vocabulary home: straight away; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding. Right away is a common AmE alternative to straight away; no new grammar decision.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `straight away` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** TIME23.

<a id="time24"></a>

### TIME24 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `First + clause; then + clause`

**Use:** Say two events occurred one after the other.

**Example:** First we checked the logs; then we restarted the service.

**Usage / decision:** Add first/then for neutral sequence; at first/eventually specifically contrasts an initial phase with a later outcome.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P098](#existing-p098).

**Replacement disposition:** [N040](cards.md#firstfirst-of-all--clause--thennextafter-that--clause--finally--clause), [N041](cards.md#the-main-point-is-that--clause--the-bottom-line-is-that--clause--at-the-end-of-the-day--clause), [N042](cards.md#to-sum-upin-summaryin-conclusionoverall--clause--long-story-short--clause)

**Sources:** TIME24.

<a id="time25"></a>

### TIME25 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `not + verb + until + time/clause / It wasn't until + time/clause + that + clause`

**Use:** Say you only noticed something after an event.

**Example:** I didn't notice the error until I got home. / It wasn't until I got home that I noticed the error.

**Usage / decision:** Both locate a delayed beginning or realization; the it-cleft emphasizes when it finally happened.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C05-043](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-043).

**Replacement disposition:** [N063](cards.md#by--deadline--until--endpoint--not--until--timeclause--it-was-not-until-x-that-y)

**Sources:** TIME25, STORY10.

## Conditions and hypotheticals

<a id="cond01"></a>

### COND01 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `If + present, will + verb`

**Use:** Make a real future plan dependent on a condition.

**Example:** If it rains, we'll stay home.

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: General if/when conditions need not use will. Only if states a necessary condition. Add to the real-condition family; unless and in case retain their own meanings.

**Added form (ADD27):** `If/When + present, present / only if + clause`

If I skip lunch, I get tired. / You can access it only if you sign in.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P084](#existing-p084); Connection evidence: [C05-028](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-028).

**Replacement disposition:** [N075](cards.md#if--present--presentwillcanmay--as-long-asprovided-that--clause--only-if--clause)

**Sources:** COND01, ADD27.

<a id="cond02"></a>

### COND02 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `If not, + clause / Otherwise, + clause`

**Use:** Give a fallback if the proposed option is unavailable.

**Example:** We can take the train if it's running. If not / Otherwise, we'll get a cab. / Save a backup; otherwise, you could lose your work.

**Usage / decision:** Make the referent explicit: We can take the train if it's running. If not, we'll get a cab. If not refers back to a condition not holding. Otherwise often gives the consequence of not following advice; both can introduce the fallback when context makes it clear.

**Replacement disposition:** [N076](cards.md#unless--clause--if-not--clause--otherwise--clause--in-that-caseif-thats-the-case--clause--thats-not-the-case)

**Sources:** COND02, COND18.

<a id="cond03"></a>

### COND03 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Unless + clause, clause`

**Use:** Warn about a consequence if a condition isn't met.

**Example:** We won't finish on time unless we get help.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C05-029](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-029).

**Replacement disposition:** [N076](cards.md#unless--clause--if-not--clause--otherwise--clause--in-that-caseif-thats-the-case--clause--thats-not-the-case)

**Sources:** COND03.

<a id="cond04"></a>

### COND04 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `If + past clause, would/could + base verb; What would you do if + past clause?`

**Use:** Describe a hypothetical present situation.

**Example:** If I had more time, I'd learn another language.

**Usage / decision:** Hypothetical choice, If I were you advice, and scenario questions reuse one conditional. If I had to choose does not necessarily indicate only a slight preference.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P085](#existing-p085).

**Replacement disposition:** [N081](cards.md#if--past-form--wouldcould--base-verb--if--subject--were-to--verb--would--base-verb--without--noun--wouldcould--base-verb)

**Sources:** COND04, COND06, COND14, CHOICE04.

<a id="cond05"></a>

### COND05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `If + had + past participle, would have + past participle`

**Use:** Describe an unreal past condition and its imagined result.

**Example:** If I'd left earlier, I wouldn't have missed the train.

**Usage / decision:** This can explain a counterfactual without expressing regret.

**Replacement disposition:** [N082](cards.md#if--had--past-participle--wouldcould-have--past-participle--if--had--past-participle--would--base-verb--now)

**Sources:** COND05.

<a id="cond07"></a>

### COND07 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `In that case, I'd + verb`

**Use:** Explain what you would do under certain circumstances.

**Example:** If the API changes, we'll need a plan. In that case, I'd update the integration first.

**Usage / decision:** In that case is a useful response chunk applied to an existing conditional, not an additional conditional system.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P084](#existing-p084), [P085](#existing-p085).

**Replacement disposition:** [N076](cards.md#unless--clause--if-not--clause--otherwise--clause--in-that-caseif-thats-the-case--clause--thats-not-the-case)

**Sources:** COND07.

<a id="cond08"></a>

### COND08 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `As long as + clause, clause`

**Use:** Say something is true only provided a condition holds.

**Example:** As long as the tests pass, we can deploy.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P044](#existing-p044).

**Replacement disposition:** [N075](cards.md#if--present--presentwillcanmay--as-long-asprovided-that--clause--only-if--clause)

**Sources:** COND08.

<a id="cond09"></a>

### COND09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Just in case + clause`

**Use:** Introduce a precaution against a possible event.

**Example:** Bring a jacket just in case it gets cold.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C05-032](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-032).

**Replacement disposition:** [N078](cards.md#in-case--clause--just-in-case--in-case-of--noun), [N079](cards.md#what-if--clause--lets-saysuppose--clause)

**Sources:** COND09.

<a id="cond10"></a>

### COND10 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `Even if + clause, clause`

**Use:** State what happens regardless of conditions.

**Example:** Even if it takes longer, I want to do it properly.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P043](#existing-p043).

**Replacement disposition:** [N077](cards.md#even-though--fact--clause--even-if--possible-case--clause--whether-or-notno-matter-whathow--clause--it-doesnt-matter--wh-if-clause)

**Sources:** COND10.

<a id="cond11"></a>

### COND11 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `depend on + noun / wh-clause; Depending on + noun / wh-clause, main clause`

**Use:** Say one thing depends on another.

**Example:** It depends on whether the tests pass. / The release depends on approval. / Depending on what we find, we may need more time.

**Usage / decision:** These express a dependency. Whether we deploy depends on the tests reverses the information order; it is not a new meaning. Depending on introduces a varying condition. Add noun complements and Depending on as variants.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P046](#existing-p046).

**Replacement disposition:** [N080](cards.md#depend-on--nounwhetherhow-clause--depending-on--nounwh-clause--main-clause--whether--clause--depends-on--nounwh-clause)

**Sources:** COND11, COND12, COND17, WORK08.

<a id="cond13"></a>

### COND13 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `What if + clause?`

**Use:** Propose a hypothetical possibility.

**Example:** What if the customer rejects the proposal?

**Replacement disposition:** [N078](cards.md#in-case--clause--just-in-case--in-case-of--noun), [N079](cards.md#what-if--clause--lets-saysuppose--clause)

**Sources:** COND13.

<a id="cond15"></a>

### COND15 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `be willing to + verb + if + clause`

**Use:** Set a condition for agreement.

**Example:** I'd be willing to move if the offer were good enough.

**Usage / decision:** A conditional offer or willingness. I'd be willing is more tentative; am willing is more direct. Neither form requires a trade-off.

**Replacement disposition:** [N093](cards.md#either-one-works-for-me--what-are-our-options-for--nounv-ing--which-would-you-recommend), [N094](cards.md#be-willingunwilling-to--verb)

**Sources:** COND15, PLAN13.

<a id="cond16"></a>

### COND16 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Without + noun, subject + would/could + verb / would/could have + past participle`

**Use:** Describe a hypothetical situation without a resource.

**Example:** Without your help, I wouldn't have finished it.

**Usage / decision:** Match the result to the time: Without your help, I wouldn't have finished it.

**Replacement disposition:** [N081](cards.md#if--past-form--wouldcould--base-verb--if--subject--were-to--verb--would--base-verb--without--noun--wouldcould--base-verb)

**Sources:** COND16.

## Cause effect and explanations

<a id="cause01"></a>

### CAUSE01 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `X caused Y to + verb`

**Use:** State that an event caused another event.

**Example:** The update caused the app to crash.

**Usage / decision:** Add cause + object + to + verb as an ordinary causative member of the existing object-infinitive family.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024).

**Replacement disposition:** [N191](cards.md#verb--object--to--verb)

**Sources:** CAUSE01.

<a id="cause02"></a>

### CAUSE02 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `X led to + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Say a situation resulted in an outcome.

**Example:** The outage led to several canceled orders.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** CAUSE02.

<a id="cause03"></a>

### CAUSE03 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `because + clause / because of or due to + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Explain what something happened because of.

**Example:** We waited because the server was down. / We waited because of the outage. / The delay was due to a configuration error.

**Usage / decision:** Same reason relationship, different complement shapes. Because of fits inside a sentence as well as in a follow-up explanation; expand the existing cause-shape card. Add because of to the existing because/due to comparison.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P007](#existing-p007).

**Replacement disposition:** [N020](cards.md#becausesinceas--clause--because-ofdue-to--nounv-ing)

**Sources:** CAUSE03, CAUSE04.

<a id="cause05"></a>

### CAUSE05 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `clause, so + result clause / As a result / Therefore / Consequently, + clause / That's why + clause`

**Use:** State something happened as a consequence.

**Example:** The server failed, so we rolled back. / The server failed. As a result, we rolled back. / The server failed. That's why we rolled back.

**Usage / decision:** Add so + result clause and therefore/consequently as alternatives; see linker design below. Added coverage: Addition, contrast, and consequence are different relationships. Integrate so with CAUSE05; add also/in addition to FLOW12. Furthermore is a more formal alternative, not another mandatory card. Addition forms remain a related expansion of FLOW12, not synonyms of result markers. One result family. So joins clauses; the sentence adverbs can begin a new sentence. That's why points back to the explanation. Therefore often marks an inference; consequently emphasizes a consequence. Do not use a comma alone to join independent clauses with however/therefore.

**Added form (ADD21):** `clause, and/but/so + clause; Also / In addition, + clause`

The tool is fast, and it's easy to use. / It failed, so we rolled back. / It supports retries. In addition, it records failures.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P010](#existing-p010); Connection evidence: [C08-001](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-001), [C08-002](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-002), [C08-006](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-006).

**Replacement disposition:** [N021](cards.md#clause-so--result--as-a-resultthereforeconsequently--clause--that-is-why--clause)

**Sources:** CAUSE05, ADD21, CAUSE06.

<a id="cause07"></a>

### CAUSE07 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `The main reason + clause + is that + clause`

**Use:** Explain the main underlying reason.

**Example:** The main reason I'm leaving is that I need a change.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P005](#existing-p005).

**Replacement disposition:** [N019](cards.md#the-main-reason--clause--is-that--clause--one-reason-for--nounv-ing--is-that--clause)

**Sources:** CAUSE07.

<a id="cause08"></a>

### CAUSE08 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `X contributed to + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Name something as a contributing cause.

**Example:** Poor communication contributed to the delay.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P072](#existing-p072).

**Replacement disposition:** [N273](cards.md#have-an-impacteffect-on--target--contribute-to--outcome--play-a-role-in--process--be-associated-with--noun)

**Sources:** CAUSE08.

<a id="cause09"></a>

### CAUSE09 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `X stems from + noun`

**Use:** Describe what a problem originates from.

**Example:** The issue stems from unclear requirements.

**Usage / decision:** Existing vocabulary home: stem from / boil down to; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-027](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-027); Vocabulary: `stem from / boil down to` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** CAUSE09.

<a id="cause10"></a>

### CAUSE10 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `It turns/turned out that + clause / subject + turns/turned out to + verb`

**Use:** Explain that a process produced an unexpected outcome.

**Example:** It turned out that the bug was in our configuration.

**Usage / decision:** A discovered fact or eventual result; it need not be caused by a process. The existing turn out / end up vocabulary note already teaches these frames. Existing vocabulary home: turn out / end up; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `turn out / end up` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** CAUSE10, STORY17.

<a id="cause11"></a>

### CAUSE11 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `end up + V-ing / adjective / in or at + place`

**Use:** Describe the eventual situation, often an unexpected one.

**Example:** I ended up taking a different route.

**Usage / decision:** The final situation, often unexpected; always is a frequency modifier, not another construction. Existing vocabulary home: turn out / end up; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `turn out / end up` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** CAUSE11, ACTION23.

<a id="cause12"></a>

### CAUSE12 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Doing X makes it + adjective + to + verb`

**Use:** Explain the effect of an action.

**Example:** Working remotely makes it easier to focus.

**Usage / decision:** Add make it + adjective + to + verb. Existing object + adjective alone does not explicitly teach anticipatory it.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P020](#existing-p020).

**Replacement disposition:** [N201](cards.md#it-is--adjective--for--person--to--verb--findmake-it--adjective--to--verb--it-is--adjective--of--person--to--verb)

**Sources:** CAUSE12.

<a id="cause13"></a>

### CAUSE13 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `prevent + person/thing + from + V-ing`

**Use:** Explain a consequence for a person.

**Example:** The outage prevented us from completing the deployment.

**Usage / decision:** The preventive-purpose example also uses to + verb. Reuse this card rather than making a second note for preventing recurrence.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P029](#existing-p029).

**Replacement disposition:** [N193](cards.md#help--person--to--verb), [N194](cards.md#preventstopkeep--person--from--v-ing)

**Sources:** CAUSE13, VERB11, PROBLEM15.

<a id="cause14"></a>

### CAUSE14 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `allow + person/thing + to + verb`

**Use:** Express what an action allowed.

**Example:** The new tool allowed us to automate testing.

**Usage / decision:** Allow can express permission or making something possible. The original setting example illustrates capability, not necessarily personal permission.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024).

**Replacement disposition:** [N191](cards.md#verb--object--to--verb)

**Sources:** CAUSE14, VERB03.

<a id="cause15"></a>

### CAUSE15 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Given + noun or that-clause, + clause`

**Use:** Describe a reason using given circumstances.

**Example:** Given the deadline, we should keep the solution simple.

**Replacement disposition:** [N022](cards.md#given--nounthat-clause--clause), [N023](cards.md#one-possible-explanation-is-that--clause--it-might-have-something-to-do-with--noun--x-has-nothing-to-do-with-y), [N024](cards.md#the-cause-of-x-is-y--what-is-causing--noun)

**Sources:** CAUSE15.

<a id="cause16"></a>

### CAUSE16 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `The idea/point/goal of X is to + verb / X is designed to + verb`

**Use:** Explain an aim, purpose, or intended function.

**Example:** The goal is to reduce repeated work. / The point of the review is to catch mistakes. / The tool is designed to catch mistakes.

**Usage / decision:** A purpose family, not exact synonyms: goal names an aim; point explains why the activity matters; designed to describes intended function without guaranteeing success. Use two small cards if the full set cannot fit naturally.

**Replacement disposition:** [N025](cards.md#in-order-to--verb--so-that--subject--cancould--verb--the-goal-is-to--verb--be-designed-to--verb)

**Sources:** CAUSE16, PLAN03, PLAN04, PLAN11.

<a id="cause17"></a>

### CAUSE17 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `The root cause is X / X isn't the cause; it's a symptom of Y`

**Use:** Highlight the real cause rather than a symptom.

**Example:** The root cause is a memory leak. / High memory use is a symptom of the leak.

**Usage / decision:** Diagnose a cause and distinguish it from an observed symptom. Compare with a matched technical example, not with every cause connector.

**Replacement disposition:** [N022](cards.md#given--nounthat-clause--clause), [N023](cards.md#one-possible-explanation-is-that--clause--it-might-have-something-to-do-with--noun--x-has-nothing-to-do-with-y), [N024](cards.md#the-cause-of-x-is-y--what-is-causing--noun)

**Sources:** CAUSE17, PROBLEM17.

<a id="cause18"></a>

### CAUSE18 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `It comes down to + noun`

**Use:** Explain something that comes down to one factor.

**Example:** The decision comes down to cost and reliability.

**Usage / decision:** Existing vocabulary home: stem from / boil down to; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding. Come down to and boil down to overlap for the essential consideration; verify whether the synonym deserves a Common patterns note.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `stem from / boil down to` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** CAUSE18.

<a id="cause19"></a>

### CAUSE19 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `It makes sense to + verb (+ because + clause) / Does it make sense to + verb?`

**Use:** Explain why a choice makes sense.

**Example:** It makes sense to wait because we lack enough data.

**Usage / decision:** The question and statement share one evaluation frame.

**Replacement disposition:** [N095](cards.md#be-worth--nounv-ing--there-is-no-point-in--v-ing--it-makes-sense-to--verb)

**Sources:** CAUSE19, QUESTION16.

## Contrast and concession

<a id="contrast01"></a>

### CONTRAST01 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `That said, / However, / Still, / Even so, + clause`

**Use:** Acknowledge a point before offering a competing one.

**Example:** The tool is useful. That said, we still need to check its output. / The tool is useful. However, it sometimes misses errors. / It sometimes misses errors. Still / Even so, it saves us time.

**Usage / decision:** All can introduce a counterpoint. That said explicitly qualifies the previous point; still and even so highlight what remains true despite it. However is a general contrast marker. Differences are tendencies, not exclusive meanings. Teach however in the grouped contrast card at CONTRAST03. These nearby alternatives may join that card if the comparison remains usable. Keep a separate That said card only if its qualification examples teach a useful distinct production target; otherwise absorb its useful coverage and retire the redundant note. Sentence-final though can join the same group.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P067](#existing-p067).

**Replacement disposition:** [N027](cards.md#that-saidstilleven-so--clause--while-it-is-true-that--clause-main-clause--x-may-be-true-but-y)

**Sources:** CONTRAST01, CONTRAST05, CONTRAST13, CONTRAST17.

<a id="contrast02"></a>

### CONTRAST02 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `(On the one hand, + clause;) on the other hand, + clause`

**Use:** Acknowledge a disadvantage despite an advantage.

**Example:** Remote work is flexible. On the other hand, it can feel isolating.

**Usage / decision:** Balances different considerations; the first half is optional when the other consideration is clear. It does not itself mean an unexpected exception.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P004](#existing-p004).

**Replacement disposition:** [N028](cards.md#clause-whereaswhile--clause--on-the-one-hand--clause-on-the-other-hand--clause)

**Sources:** CONTRAST02, CONTRAST08.

<a id="contrast03"></a>

### CONTRAST03 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `clause, but clause / Although / Though / Even though + clause, main clause / Despite / In spite of + noun or V-ing, main clause / statement. However, statement.`

**Use:** Express contrast with an unexpected outcome.

**Example:** I was tired, but I finished the review. / Although / Though / Even though I was tired, I finished the review. / Despite / In spite of being tired, I finished the review. / I was tired. However, I finished the review.

**Usage / decision:** Put these alternatives on one grouped card, expanding/replacing P042. All express concession in these matched examples. Although and though overlap; even though adds emphasis. Despite and in spite of are near-interchangeable. Teach the restructuring explicitly: but joins clauses; although introduces a dependent clause; despite takes a noun/V-ing phrase; however links statements and cannot join two independent clauses with only a comma. But also has broader uses, so this is shared use rather than complete synonymy. Preserve P043's fact-versus-hypothesis decision in the replacement because even if changes the relationship. Sentence-final though works like a conversational however; although and even though cannot replace it in that position. Include this useful position choice in the group if manageable. Coordinate with CONTRAST01 to avoid a second however card.

**Added form (ADD24):** `clause, though`

It's expensive. I'd still buy it, though. / I liked the hotel. The room was small, though.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P042](#existing-p042), [P043](#existing-p043), [P067](#existing-p067); Connection evidence: [C05-015](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-015).

**Replacement disposition:** [N026](cards.md#clause-but--clause--althoughthougheven-though--clause-main-clause--despitein-spite-of--nounv-ing-main-clause--statement-however-statement--clause-though)

**Sources:** CONTRAST03, CONTRAST04, CONTRAST12, ADD24.

<a id="contrast06"></a>

### CONTRAST06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `clause, whereas/while + clause`

**Use:** Compare contrasting facts.

**Example:** I prefer coffee, whereas she prefers tea.

**Usage / decision:** While also marks simultaneous time; whereas explicitly marks contrast. Neither necessarily describes an unexpected result.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C05-024](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-024), [C05-025](../connections/05-conjunction-clause-linkers.md#c05-025).

**Replacement disposition:** [N028](cards.md#clause-whereaswhile--clause--on-the-one-hand--clause-on-the-other-hand--clause)

**Sources:** CONTRAST06.

<a id="contrast07"></a>

### CONTRAST07 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `The only downside is that + clause`

**Use:** Introduce an exception to a positive statement.

**Example:** The only downside is that the commute is long.

**Usage / decision:** The only downside claims there is just one; one disadvantage does not. Add the useful wording without implying equivalence.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P016](#existing-p016).

**Replacement disposition:** [N141](cards.md#one-advantagedisadvantage-of--nounv-ing--is-that--clause--the-downside-is-that--clause--x-comes-at-the-cost-of-y)

**Sources:** CONTRAST07.

<a id="contrast09"></a>

### CONTRAST09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `It's not that + clause; it's that + clause / The problem isn't X; it's Y`

**Use:** Correct a misleading interpretation.

**Example:** It's not that I dislike the tool; it's that I don't trust its output yet. / The problem isn't speed; it's accuracy.

**Usage / decision:** Correct a diagnosis or interpretation. The first form explains motivation or circumstances; the second explicitly identifies the problem. Don't equate either with merely adding a contrasting consideration.

**Replacement disposition:** [N031](cards.md#it-is-not-that-x-it-is-just-that-y--its-not-like--clause--its-not-like--person--to--verb--i-am-not-saying-x-i-am-saying-y--the-problem-is-not-x-it-is-y)

**Sources:** CONTRAST09, CONTRAST10.

<a id="contrast11"></a>

### CONTRAST11 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Surprisingly, + clause`

**Use:** Mark a result as surprising.

**Example:** Surprisingly, the smaller model performed better.

**Usage / decision:** A useful optional discourse adverb, not a separate concession system.

**Replacement disposition:** [N072](cards.md#luckilyfortunatelyunfortunately--clause--to-make-matters-worse--clause), [N073](cards.md#surprisingly--clause--i-could-not-believe--clause), [N074](cards.md#almostnearly--verb--almostnearly-allevery--noun)

**Sources:** CONTRAST11.

<a id="contrast14"></a>

### CONTRAST14 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `X may be true, but Y`

**Use:** State two facts that both hold despite contrast.

**Example:** It may be cheaper, but it's less reliable.

**Usage / decision:** May be true, but offers a related concessive frame. May can acknowledge a point without expressing literal uncertainty.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P068](#existing-p068).

**Replacement disposition:** [N027](cards.md#that-saidstilleven-so--clause--while-it-is-true-that--clause-main-clause--x-may-be-true-but-y)

**Sources:** CONTRAST14.

<a id="contrast15"></a>

### CONTRAST15 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Just because + clause + doesn't mean + clause`

**Use:** Say something isn't necessarily implied by another thing.

**Example:** Just because it's popular doesn't mean it's good.

**Usage / decision:** Add Just because + clause + doesn't mean + clause as the conversational frame.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P066](#existing-p066).

**Replacement disposition:** [N030](cards.md#x-does-not-necessarily-mean-y--just-because--clause--does-not-mean--clause--x-does-not-guarantee-y)

**Sources:** CONTRAST15.

<a id="contrast16"></a>

### CONTRAST16 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `To be fair, + clause`

**Use:** Concede a fact while qualifying its importance.

**Example:** To be fair, they did warn us about the delay.

**Replacement disposition:** [N029](cards.md#i-agreedisagree-with--noun--i-agree-to-some-extent-but--clause--i-see-your-point-but--clause--that-is-a-valid-concern--to-be-fair--clause--i-have-a-different-take-on--noun)

**Sources:** CONTRAST16.

## Opinions certainty and evidence

<a id="view01"></a>

### VIEW01 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `I think / I believe / I'd say / I guess + clause`

**Use:** Give a straightforward opinion.

**Example:** I think / I believe this approach will work. / I'd say this is our best option. / I guess we can try it.

**Usage / decision:** All can express a view. I think is a neutral default; I believe can sound more deliberate; I'd say presents a judgment; I guess can signal a tentative inference or reluctant acceptance. No fixed confidence ranking: context and tone matter. Expand the judgment family; do not delete a well-learned note just to change its wording.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P054](#existing-p054).

**Replacement disposition:** [N001](cards.md#i-thinkbelievefeel-that--clause--i-do-not-thinkbelieve--clause), [N002](cards.md#in-my-opinionview--clause--from-my-perspectivepoint-of-view--clause--personally--clause--as-far-as-i-am-concerned--clause--if-you-ask-me--clause), [N003](cards.md#i-would-say--clause--i-guesssupposeimagine--clause), [N004](cards.md#i-betill-bet--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW01, VIEW02, VIEW03, VIEW04.

<a id="view06"></a>

### VIEW06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'm assuming + clause`

**Use:** Treat something as true until verified.

**Example:** I'm assuming the API remains backward-compatible.

**Replacement disposition:** [N009](cards.md#i-assumepresume--clause--assuming-that--clause-main-clause), [N010](cards.md#i-suspect--clause--i-have-a-feeling-that--clause), [N011](cards.md#i-am-surecertainconvincedconfident-that--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW06.

<a id="view07"></a>

### VIEW07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'm convinced / I'm confident + (that) clause`

**Use:** Express strong conviction.

**Example:** I'm convinced the configuration caused the failure. / I'm confident we can fix it today.

**Usage / decision:** Both express confidence. Convinced emphasizes a belief accepted as true; confident commonly expresses trust in success or ability. They overlap; compare natural contexts rather than inventing a percentage scale.

**Replacement disposition:** [N009](cards.md#i-assumepresume--clause--assuming-that--clause-main-clause), [N010](cards.md#i-suspect--clause--i-have-a-feeling-that--clause), [N011](cards.md#i-am-surecertainconvincedconfident-that--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW07, VIEW08.

<a id="view09"></a>

### VIEW09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'm not sure whether + clause`

**Use:** Express uncertainty clearly.

**Example:** I'm not sure whether this solution will scale.

**Replacement disposition:** [N013](cards.md#i-am-not-sure-whetherif--clause--i-have-no-idea--wh-whether-clause--i-am-not-entirely-convinced-that--clause--i-could-be-wrong-but--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW09.

<a id="view10"></a>

### VIEW10 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `There's a chance that + clause`

**Use:** Express an uncertain possibility.

**Example:** There's a chance that the flight will be delayed.

**Usage / decision:** Chance and possibility overlap in this probability frame.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P012](#existing-p012).

**Replacement disposition:** [N014](cards.md#maymightcould--base-verb--there-is-a-chancepossibility-that--clause--be-likelyunlikely-to--verb--be-bound-to--verb)

**Sources:** VIEW10.

<a id="view11"></a>

### VIEW11 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `The data suggests that + clause`

**Use:** State what the available data indicates.

**Example:** The data suggests that latency has increased.

**Usage / decision:** Add data/evidence as grammatical subjects; distinguish suggest from prove.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P087](#existing-p087).

**Replacement disposition:** [N015](cards.md#there-is-evidence-that--clause--the-resultsevidence-suggest-that--clause), [N016](cards.md#according-to--source--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW11.

<a id="view12"></a>

### VIEW12 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `It seems / appears that + clause / It seems / looks like + clause`

**Use:** Separate evidence from fact.

**Example:** It seems / appears that the server restarted. / It looks like the server restarted.

**Usage / decision:** All present an impression or inference; looks like often refers to apparent evidence, and appears can sound more formal. None guarantees strong evidence. Expand the existing impression card, preserving to me as an optional viewpoint. Add impersonal and like forms to the impression family.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P055](#existing-p055).

**Replacement disposition:** [N006](cards.md#it-seemsappears-that--clause--it-seemslookssoundsfeels-like--clause), [N007](cards.md#looksoundfeeltastesmell--adjective--looksoundfeeltastesmell-like--noun), [N008](cards.md#what-does-x-look-like--how-does-x-look)

**Sources:** VIEW12, VIEW13, DESCRIBE03.

<a id="view14"></a>

### VIEW14 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I have a feeling that + clause`

**Use:** Express a hunch without strong evidence.

**Example:** I have a feeling that something's wrong.

**Replacement disposition:** [N009](cards.md#i-assumepresume--clause--assuming-that--clause-main-clause), [N010](cards.md#i-suspect--clause--i-have-a-feeling-that--clause), [N011](cards.md#i-am-surecertainconvincedconfident-that--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW14.

<a id="view15"></a>

### VIEW15 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'm starting to realize / notice that + clause`

**Use:** Say you're beginning to recognize something.

**Example:** I'm starting to realize that this will take longer.

**Usage / decision:** Realize emphasizes coming to understand; notice emphasizes becoming aware through attention or observation. Often both fit. Starting to adds development, not a separate word meaning.

**Replacement disposition:** [N260](cards.md#startbegin--to--verb--v-ing)

**Sources:** VIEW15, LEARN12.

<a id="view16"></a>

### VIEW16 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I can see why + clause`

**Use:** Acknowledge someone's position.

**Example:** I can see why you're worried about the cost.

**Replacement disposition:** [N125](cards.md#i-can-see-why--clause--do-you-want-to-talk-about-it--i-am-sorry-to-hear-that--clause), [N126](cards.md#be-comfortableuncomfortable--v-ing--be-comfortableuncomfortable-with--noun), [N116](cards.md#please-do-not--verb--i-need-you-to--verb--i-need--noun--from--person)

**Sources:** VIEW16.

<a id="view17"></a>

### VIEW17 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `I see your point, but + clause`

**Use:** Disagree without dismissing someone.

**Example:** I see your point, but I think we should test it first.

**Usage / decision:** I see your point acknowledges it; I agree to some extent actually expresses partial agreement. Compare on one small acknowledgment card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P069](#existing-p069).

**Replacement disposition:** [N029](cards.md#i-agreedisagree-with--noun--i-agree-to-some-extent-but--clause--i-see-your-point-but--clause--that-is-a-valid-concern--to-be-fair--clause--i-have-a-different-take-on--noun)

**Sources:** VIEW17.

<a id="view18"></a>

### VIEW18 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `I could be wrong, but + clause`

**Use:** Indicate your conclusion may be wrong.

**Example:** I could be wrong, but I think that's the wrong version.

**Usage / decision:** I could be wrong limits confidence; add it as a softening example, not a compulsory filler.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P054](#existing-p054).

**Replacement disposition:** [N013](cards.md#i-am-not-sure-whetherif--clause--i-have-no-idea--wh-whether-clause--i-am-not-entirely-convinced-that--clause--i-could-be-wrong-but--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW18.

<a id="view19"></a>

### VIEW19 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `As far as I know, + clause`

**Use:** State a limit to your certainty.

**Example:** As far as I know, the issue hasn't been resolved.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P057](#existing-p057).

**Replacement disposition:** [N005](cards.md#in-my-experience--clause--as-far-as-i-know--clause--from-what-i-have-heard--clause--apparently--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW19.

<a id="view20"></a>

### VIEW20 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `In my experience, + clause`

**Use:** Ground an observation in personal experience.

**Example:** In my experience, smaller teams make decisions faster.

**Usage / decision:** In my experience identifies a source, not a division between fact and opinion.

**Replacement disposition:** [N005](cards.md#in-my-experience--clause--as-far-as-i-know--clause--from-what-i-have-heard--clause--apparently--clause)

**Sources:** VIEW20.

## Purpose plans and decisions

<a id="plan01"></a>

### PLAN01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `try to + verb / try + V-ing`

**Use:** State your goal in doing something.

**Example:** I tried to reconnect, but the network was down. / I tried restarting the router to see whether that would help.

**Usage / decision:** Try to emphasizes attempting a goal; try V-ing suggests testing a method. Both can describe the same act from different viewpoints. Keep a small matched contrast, not separate cards for each tense or Let's.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-098](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-098).

**Replacement disposition:** [N097](cards.md#try-to--verb--try--v-ing), [N098](cards.md#mean-to--verb--mean--v-ing)

**Sources:** PLAN01, ACTION06, ACTION07, PLAN18.

<a id="plan02"></a>

### PLAN02 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `I did X so that + clause`

**Use:** Explain why you chose an action.

**Example:** I left early so that I wouldn't miss the train.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P045](#existing-p045).

**Replacement disposition:** [N025](cards.md#in-order-to--verb--so-that--subject--cancould--verb--the-goal-is-to--verb--be-designed-to--verb)

**Sources:** PLAN02.

<a id="plan05"></a>

### PLAN05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `decide to + verb / decide against + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Explain a decision you've reached.

**Example:** We decided to migrate. / We decided against migrating this month.

**Usage / decision:** Opposite decisions with different complements. Deciding against something does not necessarily mean avoiding a risk.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-081](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-081).

**Replacement disposition:** [N086](cards.md#plandecidehopeagreepromise--to--verb--decide-against--nounv-ing--think-about--v-ing)

**Sources:** PLAN05, PLAN09.

<a id="plan06"></a>

### PLAN06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I've been thinking about + V-ing`

**Use:** Express something you've been considering.

**Example:** I've been thinking about moving abroad.

**Replacement disposition:** [N086](cards.md#plandecidehopeagreepromise--to--verb--decide-against--nounv-ing--think-about--v-ing)

**Sources:** PLAN06.

<a id="plan07"></a>

### PLAN07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `plan to + verb / be planning to + verb`

**Use:** Mention something you're planning to do.

**Example:** I'm planning to visit California next year.

**Usage / decision:** Affirmative and negative intentions belong together. Add the reason after but when the plan changes. Added coverage: Arrangements, intentions/evidence-based predictions, and offers/decisions/predictions overlap. These are contextual preferences, not absolute tense rules. Integrate PLAN07 and TIME18; separate planning from prediction if the Back grows too long.

**Added form (ADD29):** `be V-ing + future time / be going to + verb / will + verb`

I'm meeting Maya tomorrow. / I'm going to apply next week. / I'll send it now.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-082](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-082).

**Replacement disposition:** [N065](cards.md#will--base-verb--be-going-to--base-verb--present-continuous--future-time--will-be--v-ing--simple-present-for-a-timetable)

**Sources:** PLAN07, STORY12, ADD29.

<a id="plan08"></a>

### PLAN08 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `be supposed to + verb`

**Use:** Describe an expectation, arrangement, or requirement.

**Example:** I'm supposed to meet her at seven.

**Usage / decision:** Expected, arranged, or required behavior. Was supposed to does not by itself mean the action failed to happen: I was supposed to call, and I did is valid.

**Replacement disposition:** [N087](cards.md#have-tohave-got-toneed-tomust--verb--do-not-have-todo-not-need-to--verb--did-not-have-to--verb--be-going-to-have-to--verb--cannotmust-not--verb--be-supposed-to--verb--be-supposed-to-be--adjectivenoun)

**Sources:** PLAN08, MODAL19.

<a id="plan10"></a>

### PLAN10 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `(In order) to + verb, main clause`

**Use:** Explain the purpose of an action.

**Example:** In order to improve reliability, we added retries.

**Usage / decision:** Ordinary purpose to is enough in many contexts. In order to can emphasize purpose; it does not introduce a factual cause.

**Replacement disposition:** [N025](cards.md#in-order-to--verb--so-that--subject--cancould--verb--the-goal-is-to--verb--be-designed-to--verb)

**Sources:** PLAN10.

<a id="plan12"></a>

### PLAN12 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `avoid / consider + V-ing`

**Use:** Describe avoiding or considering an action, using the shared V-ing complement.

**Example:** I want to avoid making the same mistake.

**Usage / decision:** These share a complement rule, not a meaning: avoid means keep from doing; consider means think about a possibility. Both are already named on the V-ing card and have connection notes. The construction is already taught. Keep the example for transfer practice; no automatic standalone production card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P040](#existing-p040); Connection evidence: [C02-089](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-089), [C02-091](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-091).

**Replacement disposition:** [N204](cards.md#enjoyavoidconsiderfinishkeep--v-ing--feel-likecannot-help--v-ing)

**Sources:** PLAN12, ACTION12, VERB12.

<a id="plan14"></a>

### PLAN14 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `I'm leaning toward + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Say you're leaning toward one option.

**Example:** I'm leaning toward taking the earlier flight.

**Usage / decision:** Existing vocabulary home: lean toward; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `lean toward` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** PLAN14.

<a id="plan15"></a>

### PLAN15 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `What matters most is + clause or noun`

**Use:** Explain which factor will determine your choice.

**Example:** What matters most is whether the service stays reliable.

**Usage / decision:** Add What matters most; same focus construction with a different lexical choice.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P060](#existing-p060).

**Replacement disposition:** [N034](cards.md#what-i-needvaluelike-most-is--nounclause--what-matters-most-is--nounclause--what-i-struggle-with-most-is--nounv-ing)

**Sources:** PLAN15.

<a id="plan16"></a>

### PLAN16 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `Before I decide, I need to + verb`

**Use:** Say you need additional information before committing.

**Example:** Before I decide, I need to compare the costs.

**Usage / decision:** A composition of before + clause and need to + verb. Reuse planning/necessity forms; no separate essential card for this one sentence.

**Original home / related coverage:** Catalog: [MODAL05](#modal05).

**Replacement disposition:** [N086](cards.md#plandecidehopeagreepromise--to--verb--decide-against--nounv-ing--think-about--v-ing)

**Sources:** PLAN16.

<a id="plan17"></a>

### PLAN17 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `I'd prioritize X over Y`

**Use:** Express a priority.

**Example:** I'd prioritize reliability over raw speed.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** PLAN17.

## Preferences choices and comparisons

<a id="choice01"></a>

### CHOICE01 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `I'd rather + verb + than + verb`

**Use:** Say you prefer one action over another.

**Example:** I'd rather walk than wait for a cab.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P083](#existing-p083).

**Replacement disposition:** [N091](cards.md#prefer--nounv-ing--to--nounv-ing--would-prefer-to--verb--would-rather--base-verb--than--base-verb--instead-of--nounv-ing--rather-than--parallel-alternative)

**Sources:** CHOICE01.

<a id="choice02"></a>

### CHOICE02 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `I prefer + V-ing + to + V-ing`

**Use:** Compare two activities you enjoy.

**Example:** I prefer cooking at home to eating out.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P082](#existing-p082).

**Replacement disposition:** [N091](cards.md#prefer--nounv-ing--to--nounv-ing--would-prefer-to--verb--would-rather--base-verb--than--base-verb--instead-of--nounv-ing--rather-than--parallel-alternative)

**Sources:** CHOICE02.

<a id="choice03"></a>

### CHOICE03 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Either one works for me`

**Use:** Say you're comfortable with any option.

**Example:** We can meet at six or seven; either one works for me.

**Replacement disposition:** [N093](cards.md#either-one-works-for-me--what-are-our-options-for--nounv-ing--which-would-you-recommend), [N094](cards.md#be-willingunwilling-to--verb)

**Sources:** CHOICE03.

<a id="choice05"></a>

### CHOICE05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Which one would you recommend?`

**Use:** Ask someone to compare two options.

**Example:** Which one would you recommend for a beginner?

**Replacement disposition:** [N093](cards.md#either-one-works-for-me--what-are-our-options-for--nounv-ing--which-would-you-recommend), [N094](cards.md#be-willingunwilling-to--verb)

**Sources:** CHOICE05.

<a id="choice06"></a>

### CHOICE06 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Is X worth + noun or V-ing?`

**Use:** Ask whether an option is worth the cost.

**Example:** Is the upgrade worth paying for?

**Usage / decision:** Be worth + V-ing already appears among useful expressions. Add the noun alternative and a concrete example if needed.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P040](#existing-p040); Connection evidence: [C03-067](../connections/03-adjective-participle-patterns.md#c03-067).

**Replacement disposition:** [N095](cards.md#be-worth--nounv-ing--there-is-no-point-in--v-ing--it-makes-sense-to--verb), [N204](cards.md#enjoyavoidconsiderfinishkeep--v-ing--feel-likecannot-help--v-ing)

**Sources:** CHOICE06.

<a id="choice07"></a>

### CHOICE07 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `X matters more to me than Y`

**Use:** Say one quality matters more than another.

**Example:** Battery life matters more to me than screen size.

**Usage / decision:** A routine comparative of importance; the meaning of matter can be handled lexically if unfamiliar.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P053](#existing-p053).

**Replacement disposition:** [N261](cards.md#x-matters-more-to--person--than-y--choose-x-over-y), [N262](cards.md#would-rather--person--past-form--would-rather--person--had--past-participle)

**Sources:** CHOICE07.

<a id="choice09"></a>

### CHOICE09 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `X outweighs Y`

**Use:** Compare relative importance of trade-offs.

**Example:** The benefits outweigh the additional cost.

**Usage / decision:** Existing vocabulary home: outweigh; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `outweigh` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** CHOICE09.

<a id="choice10"></a>

### CHOICE10 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `I'd choose X over Y`

**Use:** State a stronger preference for one option.

**Example:** I'd choose this model over the larger one.

**Usage / decision:** Choose X over Y expresses the selected option rather than necessarily a strong preference; keep as an alternative example or vocabulary pattern.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P082](#existing-p082), [P083](#existing-p083).

**Replacement disposition:** [N261](cards.md#x-matters-more-to--person--than-y--choose-x-over-y), [N262](cards.md#would-rather--person--past-form--would-rather--person--had--past-participle)

**Sources:** CHOICE10.

**Pattern:** `X best fits + noun`

**Use:** Explain which option best meets a requirement.

**Example:** This plan best fits our budget.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Pattern:** `X is better suited for + noun`

**Use:** State something is more suitable.

**Example:** This tool is better suited for small projects.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** CHOICE12.

<a id="choice13"></a>

### CHOICE13 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `X was better than I expected`

**Use:** Say an outcome was different from expectations.

**Example:** The hotel was better than I expected.

### CHOICE14 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `They're about the same in terms of + noun`
