## Requests offers and politeness

<a id="request01"></a>

### REQUEST01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Could you + verb? / I was wondering if you could + verb / Would it be possible to + verb?`

**Use:** Request an action directly or more indirectly.

**Example:** Could you review this today? / I was wondering if you could review this today. / Would it be possible to review this today?

**Usage / decision:** Practical request alternatives. Could you addresses the actor; would it be possible asks about feasibility and can function indirectly as a request. I was wondering softens the approach. Could I ask you a favor? is an optional opening, not another essential grammar card.

**Replacement disposition:** [N109](cards.md#could-you--verb--i-was-wondering-if-you-could--verb--would-it-be-possible-to--verb--could-i-ask-you-a-favor--i-would-be-grateful-if-you-could--verb)

**Sources:** REQUEST01, REQUEST02, QUESTION15, REQUEST08.

<a id="request03"></a>

### REQUEST03 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Do you mind if I + clause? / Would you mind + V-ing? / Is it okay if I + clause?`

**Use:** Ask permission for your action or ask another person to act.

**Example:** Do you mind if I open the window? — No, go ahead. / Would you mind opening the window? — Sure. / Is it okay if I leave early? — Yes.

**Usage / decision:** The first and third ask permission for my action; Would you mind doing asks you to act. No, not at all grants permission after mind; Yes, that's fine answers okay. Tone and context matter.

**Replacement disposition:** [N110](cards.md#cancould-i--verb--do-you-mind-if--present-clause--would-you-mind--v-ing--is-it-okay-if--clause--would-you-mind-if--past-clause)

**Sources:** REQUEST03, REQUEST04, REQUEST18.

<a id="request05"></a>

### REQUEST05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Do you want me to + verb? / Do you need a hand with + noun?`

**Use:** Offer assistance naturally.

**Example:** Do you want me to carry that for you?

**Usage / decision:** Both offer help; the first names an action, the second a task. They are alternatives, not different degrees of willingness.

**Replacement disposition:** [N111](cards.md#do-you-want-me-to--verb--do-you-need-a-hand-with--noun--what-would-you-like-me-to--verb)

**Sources:** REQUEST05, QUESTION17.

<a id="request06"></a>

### REQUEST06 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `take care of + noun`

**Use:** Offer to take responsibility for something.

**Example:** I can take care of the reservation.

**Usage / decision:** Same lexical chunk across offers, commitments, and task reports; can and will change modality, not the chunk. Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** REQUEST06, WORK16, CHUNK06.

<a id="request07"></a>

### REQUEST07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Are you free + time / to + verb? / Does + time + work for you? / Could we move + event + to + time?`

**Use:** Check availability, propose a time, or change an arrangement.

**Example:** Are you free on Friday? / Does two o'clock work for you? / Could we move the meeting to Monday?

**Usage / decision:** Availability, proposing a time, and rescheduling form one planning task. Compare the jobs explicitly; the forms are not interchangeable.

**Replacement disposition:** [N112](cards.md#are-you-free--timeto--verb--does--time--work-for-you--could-we-move--event--to--time--i-cant-make-it-to--event--can-we-make-it--time--when-will-you-be-ready-to--verb--do-you-have-any-plans-for--time)

**Sources:** REQUEST07, ADD08.

<a id="request09"></a>

### REQUEST09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Could you say/repeat + part + again? / Could you slow down a little?`

**Use:** Ask someone to repeat themselves.

**Example:** Could you repeat the last part? / Could you slow down a little?

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: REQUEST09 owns repetition. Add a concrete remote-call context; breaking up here describes intermittent audio, not ending a relationship. Repair a listening problem: repeat missing information versus reduce speaking speed. Keep the audio-problem context from ADD15.

**Added form (ADD15):** `You're breaking up / I couldn't catch + part / Could you repeat + part?`

You're breaking up. Could you repeat the last part? / I couldn't catch the date.

**Replacement disposition:** [N221](cards.md#could-you-sayrepeat--noun--again--could-you-slow-down--what-do-you-mean-by--noun--could-you-clarify--wh-clause)

**Sources:** REQUEST09, ADD15, REQUEST10.

<a id="request11"></a>

### REQUEST11 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `Could you clarify what you mean by + noun? / What do you mean by + noun?`

**Use:** Politely ask for clarification.

**Example:** Could you clarify what you mean by 'ready'?

**Usage / decision:** Both ask for meaning. The direct question can sound challenging depending on tone; the longer form is useful in professional discussions.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P101](#existing-p101).

**Replacement disposition:** [N221](cards.md#could-you-sayrepeat--noun--again--could-you-slow-down--what-do-you-mean-by--noun--could-you-clarify--wh-clause)

**Sources:** REQUEST11, FLOW07.

<a id="request12"></a>

### REQUEST12 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Just to confirm, + statement? / So you're saying that + clause?`

**Use:** Ask to double-check an understanding.

**Example:** Just to confirm, we're meeting at three?

**Usage / decision:** Check a shared understanding. Just to confirm often checks arrangements; So you're saying restates an interpretation. Both can be examples of the existing understanding-check card. Keep one understanding-check family with practical alternatives.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P100](#existing-p100).

**Replacement disposition:** [N222](cards.md#if-i-understand-correctly--clause--just-to-confirm--clause--so-you-are-saying-that--clause--does-that-make-sense--are-we-on-the-same-page-about--noun)

**Sources:** REQUEST12, FLOW17.

<a id="request13"></a>

### REQUEST13 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Thanks for asking, but + clause`

**Use:** Decline an invitation politely.

**Example:** Thanks for asking, but I can't make it tonight.

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: Expand REQUEST13 with a reason or alternative; don't pretend inability and lack of interest mean the same thing.

**Added form (ADD10):** `I'd love to, but + clause / I can't + verb, but I can + alternative`

I'd love to, but I'm working that evening. / I can't review it today, but I can look tomorrow.

**Replacement disposition:** [N115](cards.md#that-would-be-great-thanks--im-good-thanks--thanks-for-asking-but--clause--id-love-to-but--clause--i-would-rather-not--verb), [N116](cards.md#please-do-not--verb--i-need-you-to--verb--i-need--noun--from--person)

**Sources:** REQUEST13, ADD10.

<a id="request14"></a>

### REQUEST14 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `That would be great, thanks`

**Use:** Politely accept an offer.

**Example:** You can pick me up at six? That would be great, thanks.

**Replacement disposition:** [N115](cards.md#that-would-be-great-thanks--im-good-thanks--thanks-for-asking-but--clause--id-love-to-but--clause--i-would-rather-not--verb), [N116](cards.md#please-do-not--verb--i-need-you-to--verb--i-need--noun--from--person)

**Sources:** REQUEST14.

<a id="request15"></a>

### REQUEST15 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `appreciate + noun / (person +) V-ing / how + clause`

**Use:** Express gratitude for someone's effort.

**Example:** I really appreciate your help. / I appreciate you helping me out. / I appreciate how much work you put into this.

**Usage / decision:** Appreciate takes the appreciated thing or action directly: I appreciate your help / you helping / how much you helped. The differing complements belong in one small gratitude family. Existing notes cover appreciate + V-ing and an explicit V-ing subject. Add a gratitude example and how-clause only if useful.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P039](#existing-p039), [P040](#existing-p040).

**Replacement disposition:** [N117](cards.md#sorry-forabout--noun--sorry-for--v-ing--that-was-my-mistake), [N118](cards.md#i-appreciate--nounv-inghow-clause--thank-you-for--nounv-ing)

**Sources:** REQUEST15, FEEL13.

<a id="request16"></a>

### REQUEST16 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Sorry for + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Apologize for inconvenience.

**Example:** Sorry for keeping you waiting.

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: Expand REQUEST16: sorry for + V-ing names the action; sorry + clause also names the actor. Pair acknowledgment with a realistic repair.

**Added form (ADD09):** `I'm sorry I + past clause. I'll + repair action`

I'm sorry I missed the meeting. I'll send my update now. / I'm sorry I sent the wrong file. I'll replace it.

**Replacement disposition:** [N117](cards.md#sorry-forabout--noun--sorry-for--v-ing--that-was-my-mistake), [N118](cards.md#i-appreciate--nounv-inghow-clause--thank-you-for--nounv-ing)

**Sources:** REQUEST16, ADD09.

<a id="request19"></a>

### REQUEST19 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Why don't we + verb? / How about + noun or V-ing?`

**Use:** Suggest making a shared plan.

**Example:** Why don't we test a smaller batch? / How about testing a smaller batch?

**Usage / decision:** Both suggest a shared option. Why don't we need not demand a literal reason. Neither is automatically pressure-free; tone matters.

**Replacement disposition:** [N113](cards.md#do-you-want-towould-you-like-to--verb--how-about--nounv-ing--why-dont-we--verb--lets--verb)

**Sources:** REQUEST19, REQUEST20.

## Clarifying and conversation flow

<a id="flow01"></a>

### FLOW01 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `What I mean is, + clause / What I'm trying to say/express is + noun or clause`

**Use:** Start explaining the core of your point.

**Example:** What I mean is, we need better monitoring.

**Usage / decision:** Same clarification job; the longer form can signal difficulty expressing the idea. Reuse the existing meaning-clarification card. Add the longer clarification wording only if useful in production.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P062](#existing-p062).

**Replacement disposition:** [N036](cards.md#what-i-mean-is--clause--what-i-am-trying-to-say-is--clause--that-is-not-what-i-meant--in-other-wordsto-put-it-simply--clause--sorry-i-meant-to-say--correction)

**Sources:** FLOW01, FLOW14, LEARN16.

<a id="flow02"></a>

### FLOW02 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `The thing is, + clause`

**Use:** Introduce a complication or important detail.

**Example:** The thing is, I don't have a car.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P061](#existing-p061).

**Replacement disposition:** [N035](cards.md#the-thing-is--clause)

**Sources:** FLOW02.

<a id="flow03"></a>

### FLOW03 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `In other words, + clause / To put it simply, + clause`

**Use:** Restate an idea more simply.

**Example:** In other words, we need to start over.

**Usage / decision:** Both can restate a point. In other words reformulates; to put it simply specifically promises simplification. Don't present them as always interchangeable. Add To put it simply as a specific simplification choice.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P065](#existing-p065).

**Replacement disposition:** [N036](cards.md#what-i-mean-is--clause--what-i-am-trying-to-say-is--clause--that-is-not-what-i-meant--in-other-wordsto-put-it-simply--clause--sorry-i-meant-to-say--correction)

**Sources:** FLOW03, FLOW04.

<a id="flow05"></a>

### FLOW05 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `That's not what I meant`

**Use:** Correct a misunderstanding.

**Example:** That's not what I meant. I was talking about the deadline.

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: Expand FLOW05 and P062 with explicit self-correction, instead of adding a second generic clarification card. Self-correction is a useful extension of meaning clarification; preserve the explicit repair examples.

**Added form (ADD14):** `Sorry, I meant X, not Y / Let me rephrase that`

Sorry, I meant Thursday, not Tuesday. / Let me rephrase that: the test passed, but the release isn't approved.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P062](#existing-p062).

**Replacement disposition:** [N036](cards.md#what-i-mean-is--clause--what-i-am-trying-to-say-is--clause--that-is-not-what-i-meant--in-other-wordsto-put-it-simply--clause--sorry-i-meant-to-say--correction)

**Sources:** FLOW05, ADD14.

<a id="flow06"></a>

### FLOW06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Does that make sense?`

**Use:** Ask whether your explanation is clear.

**Example:** We need to test it separately. Does that make sense?

**Replacement disposition:** [N045](cards.md#can-i-jump-in-for-a-second--let-me-finish--noun), [N046](cards.md#let-me-think-for-a-second--i-have-never-really-thought-about--nounwh-clause--i-am-not-sure-how-to-answer-that--its-hard-to-say--wh-whether-clause), [N222](cards.md#if-i-understand-correctly--clause--just-to-confirm--clause--so-you-are-saying-that--clause--does-that-make-sense--are-we-on-the-same-page-about--noun)

**Sources:** FLOW06.

<a id="flow08"></a>

### FLOW08 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Going back to + topic/what someone said, + clause / Anyway, as I was saying, + clause`

**Use:** Refer back to a previous remark.

**Example:** Going back to what you said, I agree about the cost.

**Usage / decision:** Both return to an earlier thread; as I was saying resumes your own interrupted point.

**Replacement disposition:** [N044](cards.md#speaking-of--noun--clause--going-back-to--topic--clause--anyway-as-i-was-saying--clause--now-that-you-mention-it--clause--one-thing-i-forgot-to-mention-is--nounclause)

**Sources:** FLOW08, FLOW10.

<a id="flow09"></a>

### FLOW09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Speaking of + noun, + clause`

**Use:** Shift to a related subject.

**Example:** Speaking of travel, have you booked your flights?

**Replacement disposition:** [N044](cards.md#speaking-of--noun--clause--going-back-to--topic--clause--anyway-as-i-was-saying--clause--now-that-you-mention-it--clause--one-thing-i-forgot-to-mention-is--nounclause)

**Sources:** FLOW09.

<a id="flow11"></a>

### FLOW11 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `For example, + clause`

**Use:** Introduce an example.

**Example:** For example, we could cache the results.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P064](#existing-p064).

**Replacement disposition:** [N037](cards.md#for-examplefor-instance--clause--such-aslike--nounv-ing), [N038](cards.md#in-factactually--clause)

**Sources:** FLOW11.

<a id="flow12"></a>

### FLOW12 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Another thing is that + clause`

**Use:** Add a supporting point.

**Example:** Another thing is that the API is changing.

**Replacement disposition:** [N039](cards.md#also--clause--in-additionfurthermore--clause--another-thing-is-that--clause--not-only-x-but-also-y)

**Sources:** FLOW12.

<a id="flow13"></a>

### FLOW13 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `The bottom line is that + clause`

**Use:** Summarize your point.

**Example:** The bottom line is that we need more time.

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: Expand FLOW13 with a neutral wrap-up. In conclusion suits a formal conclusion but can sound heavy in a brief conversation.

**Added form (ADD22):** `Overall, + judgment / For those reasons, I'd + recommendation`

Overall, the smaller model is a better fit. / For those reasons, I'd start with a pilot.

**Replacement disposition:** [N040](cards.md#firstfirst-of-all--clause--thennextafter-that--clause--finally--clause), [N041](cards.md#the-main-point-is-that--clause--the-bottom-line-is-that--clause--at-the-end-of-the-day--clause), [N042](cards.md#to-sum-upin-summaryin-conclusionoverall--clause--long-story-short--clause)

**Sources:** FLOW13, ADD22.

<a id="flow15"></a>

### FLOW15 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Can I jump in for a second?`

**Use:** Politely interrupt a discussion.

**Example:** Can I jump in for a second? I have a quick question.

**Replacement disposition:** [N045](cards.md#can-i-jump-in-for-a-second--let-me-finish--noun), [N046](cards.md#let-me-think-for-a-second--i-have-never-really-thought-about--nounwh-clause--i-am-not-sure-how-to-answer-that--its-hard-to-say--wh-whether-clause), [N222](cards.md#if-i-understand-correctly--clause--just-to-confirm--clause--so-you-are-saying-that--clause--does-that-make-sense--are-we-on-the-same-page-about--noun)

**Sources:** FLOW15.

<a id="flow16"></a>

### FLOW16 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Let me think for a second`

**Use:** Signal uncertainty while thinking aloud.

**Example:** Let me think for a second. I haven't tried that.

**Replacement disposition:** [N045](cards.md#can-i-jump-in-for-a-second--let-me-finish--noun), [N046](cards.md#let-me-think-for-a-second--i-have-never-really-thought-about--nounwh-clause--i-am-not-sure-how-to-answer-that--its-hard-to-say--wh-whether-clause), [N222](cards.md#if-i-understand-correctly--clause--just-to-confirm--clause--so-you-are-saying-that--clause--does-that-make-sense--are-we-on-the-same-page-about--noun)

**Sources:** FLOW16.

<a id="flow18"></a>

### FLOW18 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Now that you mention it, + clause`

**Use:** Respond to something that triggers a thought or recollection.

**Example:** Now that you mention it, we should check the logs.

**Usage / decision:** Now that you mention it does not inherently signal reconsidering a decision.

**Replacement disposition:** [N044](cards.md#speaking-of--noun--clause--going-back-to--topic--clause--anyway-as-i-was-saying--clause--now-that-you-mention-it--clause--one-thing-i-forgot-to-mention-is--nounclause)

**Sources:** FLOW18.

## Everyday actions and experiences

<a id="action01"></a>

### ACTION01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I managed to + verb`

**Use:** Say you managed to accomplish something difficult.

**Example:** I managed to catch the last train.

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: Could commonly describes general past ability; was able to/managed to can report actual success in a particular event. Don't claim could is never possible for specific events (perception and negative forms matter). Combine ACTION01 with MODAL09 only as a reviewed small contrast. Use be able to after another modal or in infinitive/perfect forms: I'll be able to help; I haven't been able to log in. Do not stack will can. Reference: [Cambridge on could](https://dictionary.cambridge.org/grammar/british-grammar/could%3A).

**Added form (ADD31):** `could + verb / was/were able to + verb / managed to + verb`

I could read English as a child. / I was able to recover the file. / I managed to recover it after several attempts.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-086](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-086), [C03-011](../connections/03-adjective-participle-patterns.md#c03-011).

**Replacement disposition:** [N096](cards.md#canbe-able-to--verb--could--verb--managed-to--verb--could-not--verb--in-time--cannot-seem-to--verb)

**Sources:** ACTION01, ADD31.

<a id="action02"></a>

### ACTION02 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I couldn't + verb + in time`

**Use:** Say you couldn't manage to do something.

**Example:** I couldn't finish in time.

**Replacement disposition:** [N096](cards.md#canbe-able-to--verb--could--verb--managed-to--verb--could-not--verb--in-time--cannot-seem-to--verb)

**Sources:** ACTION02.

<a id="action03"></a>

### ACTION03 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `remember/forget to + verb / remember/forget + V-ing`

**Use:** Say you forgot to perform an action.

**Example:** I remembered to save the file. / I remember saving it. / I forgot to send the email. / I'll never forget meeting her.

**Usage / decision:** To + verb concerns remembering or forgetting to perform an action; V-ing concerns memory of an experience. I'll never forget doing is especially natural for a memorable experience.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-096](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-096).

**Replacement disposition:** [N100](cards.md#rememberforget--to--verb--rememberforget--v-ing), [N101](cards.md#stop--v-ing--stop--to--verb)

**Sources:** ACTION03, ACTION04, ACTION05.

<a id="action08"></a>

### ACTION08 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `stop + V-ing / stop + to + verb`

**Use:** Say you stopped one activity before another.

**Example:** I stopped checking email. / I stopped to check my email.

**Usage / decision:** Stop V-ing ends the named activity; stop to V interrupts another activity for a purpose. Neither requires a habit, and stop V-ing does not imply a later action.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-099](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-099).

**Replacement disposition:** [N100](cards.md#rememberforget--to--verb--rememberforget--v-ing), [N101](cards.md#stop--v-ing--stop--to--verb)

**Sources:** ACTION08, ACTION09, PROBLEM04.

<a id="action10"></a>

### ACTION10 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `keep + V-ing`

**Use:** Describe an action continuing or happening repeatedly.

**Example:** I kept waking up during the night.

**Usage / decision:** Continuing or repeated activity; neither unexpected duration nor annoyance is required. The construction is already taught. Keep the example for transfer practice; no automatic standalone production card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P040](#existing-p040); Connection evidence: [C02-092](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-092).

**Replacement disposition:** [N204](cards.md#enjoyavoidconsiderfinishkeep--v-ing--feel-likecannot-help--v-ing)

**Sources:** ACTION10, PROBLEM05.

<a id="action11"></a>

### ACTION11 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `have trouble/difficulty + V-ing / have trouble with + noun`

**Use:** Say you have difficulty doing something.

**Example:** I have trouble remembering names.

**Usage / decision:** Trouble and difficulty overlap here. V-ing names the difficult action; with introduces the problematic thing. Add trouble as an alternative to the existing difficulty card. Add trouble + V-ing and trouble with + noun.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P018](#existing-p018).

**Replacement disposition:** [N104](cards.md#have-troubledifficulty--v-ing--have-trouble-with--noun), [N105](cards.md#be-done--v-ing--with--noun--still-need-to--verb)

**Sources:** ACTION11, PROBLEM02.

<a id="action13"></a>

### ACTION13 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `I happened to + verb`

**Use:** Describe an unplanned discovery.

**Example:** I happened to see your message while I was outside.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** ACTION13.

<a id="action14"></a>

### ACTION14 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `subject + accidentally + verb`

**Use:** Describe an accidental action.

**Example:** I accidentally deleted the wrong file.

**Usage / decision:** One adverb pattern across subjects and tenses; accidental actions need not be past. Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** ACTION14, STORY06.

<a id="action15"></a>

### ACTION15 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I didn't mean to + verb`

**Use:** Describe something you didn't intend to do.

**Example:** I didn't mean to interrupt you.

**Replacement disposition:** [N097](cards.md#try-to--verb--try--v-ing), [N098](cards.md#mean-to--verb--mean--v-ing)

**Sources:** ACTION15.

<a id="action16"></a>

### ACTION16 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `I can't help + V-ing`

**Use:** Say you can't resist doing something.

**Example:** I can't help laughing when he does that.

**Usage / decision:** The construction is already taught. Keep the example for transfer practice; no automatic standalone production card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P040](#existing-p040).

**Replacement disposition:** [N204](cards.md#enjoyavoidconsiderfinishkeep--v-ing--feel-likecannot-help--v-ing)

**Sources:** ACTION16.

<a id="action17"></a>

### ACTION17 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `be done + V-ing / be done with + noun`

**Use:** Say you're finished with an activity.

**Example:** I'm done packing.

**Usage / decision:** Both describe completion. Almost changes degree, not the construction; done with a person can instead imply wanting no further involvement.

**Replacement disposition:** [N104](cards.md#have-troubledifficulty--v-ing--have-trouble-with--noun), [N105](cards.md#be-done--v-ing--with--noun--still-need-to--verb)

**Sources:** ACTION17, AMOUNT11.

<a id="action18"></a>

### ACTION18 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `I still need to + verb`

**Use:** Say you still have a task to complete.

**Example:** I still need to buy groceries.

**Usage / decision:** Still + need to is an application of the obligation/necessity family MODAL05; keep as transfer practice.

**Original home / related coverage:** Catalog: [MODAL05](#modal05).

**Replacement disposition:** [N104](cards.md#have-troubledifficulty--v-ing--have-trouble-with--noun), [N105](cards.md#be-done--v-ing--with--noun--still-need-to--verb)

**Sources:** ACTION18.

<a id="action19"></a>

### ACTION19 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `It takes/took + (person +) time/effort + to + verb / How long does it take to + verb? / How much longer will + subject + verb?`

**Use:** State or ask about time or effort needed.

**Example:** It took me two hours to get home. / It takes patience to debug this. / How long does it take to run the tests?

**Usage / decision:** Describe time or effort needed. It took two hours does not inherently mean longer than expected. The question is the same time-needed construction. A neutral duration does not mean the task took longer than expected. How long asks total duration; how much longer asks remaining duration. The original ETA template needed subject + base verb after will.

**Replacement disposition:** [N106](cards.md#it-takes--person--timeeffort--to--verb--spend--timemoney--on--noun--v-ing--how-much-longer-will--clause)

**Sources:** ACTION19, ACTION20, QUESTION02, REQUEST17.

<a id="action21"></a>

### ACTION21 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `have/get a chance to + verb / Have you had a chance to + verb?`

**Use:** Explain your reason for not doing something.

**Example:** Have you had a chance to review it? / I haven't had a chance to read it yet.

**Usage / decision:** Opportunity or a polite check on completion. Compare asking whether someone has done the task with reporting that you lacked an opportunity.

**Replacement disposition:** [N108](cards.md#haveget-a-chance-to--verb--give--person--a-chancethe-opportunity-to--verb--have-you-had-a-chance-to--verb)

**Sources:** ACTION21, QUESTION14.

<a id="action22"></a>

### ACTION22 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `I tend to + verb`

**Use:** Describe something that happens repeatedly.

**Example:** I tend to overthink things.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P056](#existing-p056).

**Replacement disposition:** [N017](cards.md#usuallyoftenrarelyhardly-ever--main-verb--be--frequency-adverb--every-now-and-then--how-often-), [N018](cards.md#tend-to--verb--in-generalgenerallyin-most-cases--clause)

**Sources:** ACTION22.

## Emotions reactions and relationships

<a id="feel01"></a>

### FEEL01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'm glad/relieved (that) + clause`

**Use:** Respond positively to good news, with relief when a worry has passed.

**Example:** I'm glad you got the job. / I'm relieved everyone got home safely.

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: FEEL01 and DESCRIBE01 already cover two forms. Add the sympathy response; it expresses concern, not necessarily an apology for responsibility. Glad broadly expresses pleasure; relieved specifically follows worry or a feared outcome. Either may fit good news, depending on the preceding concern.

**Added form (ADD12):** `I'm sorry to hear that / That sounds + adjective / I'm glad + clause`

I'm sorry to hear that. / That sounds frustrating. / I'm glad the interview went well.

**Replacement disposition:** [N122](cards.md#i-am-gladrelieved-that--clause--i-am-gladrelieved-to--verb), [N123](cards.md#i-am-worriedconcerned-that--clause--i-am-worriedconcerned-about--nounv-ing--it-concerns-me-that--clause--my-concern-is-that--clause), [N124](cards.md#i-feel--adjective--when--clause--it-bothers-me-thatwhen--clause--i-love-ithate-itcant-stand-it-when--clause)

## Describing people objects and situations

<a id="describe01"></a>

### DESCRIBE01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `look/sound/feel + adjective`

**Use:** Describe appearance, an impression from sound or a report, or a sensation.

**Example:** You look tired. / That sounds exhausting. / I feel tired.

**Usage / decision:** Describe appearance, audible or reported impression, and experienced sensation respectively. That sounds exhausting can respond to someone's account. The shared complement is an adjective; the senses still differ.

**Replacement disposition:** [N006](cards.md#it-seemsappears-that--clause--it-seemslookssoundsfeels-like--clause), [N007](cards.md#looksoundfeeltastesmell--adjective--looksoundfeeltastesmell-like--noun), [N008](cards.md#what-does-x-look-like--how-does-x-look)

**Sources:** DESCRIBE01, DESCRIBE16, FEEL11.

<a id="describe02"></a>

### DESCRIBE02 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `look like + noun / resemble + noun`

**Use:** Compare how something appears to another object.

**Example:** This design looks like / resembles the old one.

**Usage / decision:** Both can describe likeness. Look like is common in conversation; resemble is more compact and somewhat more formal. Don't add like after resemble. Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-013](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-013).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** DESCRIBE02, DESCRIBE04.

<a id="describe07"></a>

### DESCRIBE07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `consist of + parts / include + item / involve + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** State what a thing consists of.

**Example:** The service consists of three components. / The plan includes support. / Maintaining it involves checking the logs.

**Usage / decision:** All relate something to a larger whole, but not as synonyms: consist of describes composition, include names a member without necessarily listing all, involve describes an activity or requirement.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-064](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-064).

## Problems mistakes and solutions

<a id="problem01"></a>

### PROBLEM01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Something went wrong with + noun`

**Use:** Introduce something that's gone wrong.

**Example:** Something went wrong with the payment.

**Replacement disposition:** [N137](cards.md#something-went-wrong-with--noun--x-is-not-working-properly--it-only-happens-when--clause), [N023](cards.md#one-possible-explanation-is-that--clause--it-might-have-something-to-do-with--noun--x-has-nothing-to-do-with-y)

**Sources:** PROBLEM01.

<a id="problem03"></a>

### PROBLEM03 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `X isn't working properly`

**Use:** Say a system isn't functioning as expected.

**Example:** My laptop isn't working properly.

**Replacement disposition:** [N137](cards.md#something-went-wrong-with--noun--x-is-not-working-properly--it-only-happens-when--clause), [N023](cards.md#one-possible-explanation-is-that--clause--it-might-have-something-to-do-with--noun--x-has-nothing-to-do-with-y)

**Sources:** PROBLEM03.

<a id="problem06"></a>

### PROBLEM06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `It only happens when + clause`

**Use:** Say a problem happens only under a condition.

**Example:** It only happens when I'm on mobile data.

**Replacement disposition:** [N137](cards.md#something-went-wrong-with--noun--x-is-not-working-properly--it-only-happens-when--clause), [N023](cards.md#one-possible-explanation-is-that--clause--it-might-have-something-to-do-with--noun--x-has-nothing-to-do-with-y)

**Sources:** PROBLEM06.

<a id="problem07"></a>

### PROBLEM07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `It might have something to do with + noun`

**Use:** Identify a possible cause without certainty.

**Example:** It might have something to do with the update.

**Replacement disposition:** [N137](cards.md#something-went-wrong-with--noun--x-is-not-working-properly--it-only-happens-when--clause), [N023](cards.md#one-possible-explanation-is-that--clause--it-might-have-something-to-do-with--noun--x-has-nothing-to-do-with-y)

**Sources:** PROBLEM07.

<a id="problem08"></a>

### PROBLEM08 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `What's causing + noun?`

**Use:** Ask about the actual cause.

**Example:** What's causing the slowdown?

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: Subject question versus object question. PROBLEM08 already supplies a subject question; use one productive contrast rather than one note for each question word.

**Added form (ADD36):** `Who/What + verb? / Who/What + do/does/did + subject + verb?`

Who called you? / Who did you call? / What caused the failure?

**Replacement disposition:** [N138](cards.md#can-you-walk-me-through--noun--let-me-walk-you-through--noun), [N139](cards.md#that-did-not-solvefix--noun--x-solvedfixed--noun), [N024](cards.md#the-cause-of-x-is-y--what-is-causing--noun)

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** WORK19.

<a id="work20"></a>

### WORK20 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `Who's responsible for + noun or V-ing?`

**Use:** Ask who has a particular duty.

**Example:** Who's responsible for approving this change?

**Usage / decision:** Responsibility does not always imply final decision-making authority; name approving if that is the duty you mean.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P075](#existing-p075).

**Replacement disposition:** [N129](cards.md#be-responsible-for--nounv-ing--my-contribution-was-to--verb--i--action-which--result)

**Sources:** WORK20.

<a id="work21"></a>

### WORK21 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Here's what we agreed on`

**Use:** Summarize a meeting's conclusions.

**Example:** Here's what we agreed on: test the fix today and deploy it tomorrow.

**Replacement disposition:** [N130](cards.md#where-are-we-with--task--could-you-give-me-an-update-on--task--i-will-keep-you-posted-on--noun), [N131](cards.md#here-is-what-we-agreed-on--the-next-step-is-to--verb)

**Sources:** WORK21.

<a id="work22"></a>

### WORK22 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Are we all on the same page?`

**Use:** Request everyone's alignment.

**Example:** Before we proceed, are we all on the same page?

**Usage / decision:** Check shared understanding or alignment, not necessarily unanimous agreement. Compare REQUEST12, which checks your interpretation; keep this as an interaction alternative rather than a claim that agreement and understanding are identical.

**Replacement disposition:** [N134](cards.md#i-would-appreciate-your-feedback-on--noun--could-you-tell-me-what-i-could-improve), [N029](cards.md#i-agreedisagree-with--noun--i-agree-to-some-extent-but--clause--i-see-your-point-but--clause--that-is-a-valid-concern--to-be-fair--clause--i-have-a-different-take-on--noun), [N222](cards.md#if-i-understand-correctly--clause--just-to-confirm--clause--so-you-are-saying-that--clause--does-that-make-sense--are-we-on-the-same-page-about--noun)

**Sources:** WORK22.

## Storytelling and reactions

<a id="story01"></a>

### STORY01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `You won't believe what happened`

**Use:** Introduce a surprising story.

**Example:** You won't believe what happened on my way home.

**Replacement disposition:** [N071](cards.md#you-will-not-believe-what-happened--so-theres-this--noun--clause--all-of-a-suddenout-of-nowhere--clause--the-next-thing-i-knew--clause)

**Sources:** STORY01.

<a id="story02"></a>

### STORY02 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `All of a sudden, + clause / Out of nowhere, + clause`

**Use:** Describe something unexpected happening suddenly.

**Example:** All of a sudden, the app froze. / A new error appeared out of nowhere.

**Usage / decision:** Both can introduce a surprising event. All of a sudden emphasizes abrupt timing; out of nowhere emphasizes an unexpected appearance or source.

**Replacement disposition:** [N071](cards.md#you-will-not-believe-what-happened--so-theres-this--noun--clause--all-of-a-suddenout-of-nowhere--clause--the-next-thing-i-knew--clause)

**Sources:** STORY02, STORY13.

<a id="story03"></a>

### STORY03 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Right after + noun or clause, + clause`

**Use:** Describe an action immediately following an event.

**Example:** Right after I got home, it started pouring.

**Replacement disposition:** [N062](cards.md#as-soon-asoncethe-moment--clause--main-clause--right-after--nounclause)

**Sources:** STORY03.

<a id="story05"></a>

### STORY05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `The next thing I knew, + clause`

**Use:** Describe what happened next.

**Example:** The next thing I knew, everyone was laughing.

**Replacement disposition:** [N071](cards.md#you-will-not-believe-what-happened--so-theres-this--noun--clause--all-of-a-suddenout-of-nowhere--clause--the-next-thing-i-knew--clause)

**Sources:** STORY05.

<a id="story07"></a>

### STORY07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I almost + past verb`

**Use:** Describe an event that nearly happened.

**Example:** I almost missed my flight.

**Usage / decision:** Almost can apply to welcome or unwelcome events: I almost won; I almost missed it.

**Replacement disposition:** [N072](cards.md#luckilyfortunatelyunfortunately--clause--to-make-matters-worse--clause), [N073](cards.md#surprisingly--clause--i-could-not-believe--clause), [N074](cards.md#almostnearly--verb--almostnearly-allevery--noun)

**Sources:** STORY07.

<a id="story08"></a>

### STORY08 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `To make matters worse, + clause`

**Use:** Say a situation became worse.

**Example:** To make matters worse, my phone died.

**Replacement disposition:** [N072](cards.md#luckilyfortunatelyunfortunately--clause--to-make-matters-worse--clause), [N073](cards.md#surprisingly--clause--i-could-not-believe--clause), [N074](cards.md#almostnearly--verb--almostnearly-allevery--noun)

**Sources:** STORY08.

<a id="story09"></a>

### STORY09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Luckily, + clause`

**Use:** Evaluate an event or outcome as fortunate.

**Example:** Luckily, no one was hurt.

**Usage / decision:** Luckily does not require an event to have been avoided.

**Replacement disposition:** [N072](cards.md#luckilyfortunatelyunfortunately--clause--to-make-matters-worse--clause), [N073](cards.md#surprisingly--clause--i-could-not-believe--clause), [N074](cards.md#almostnearly--verb--almostnearly-allevery--noun)

**Sources:** STORY09.

<a id="story11"></a>

### STORY11 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `In the end, + clause`

**Use:** Give the final outcome.

**Example:** In the end, we decided not to go.

**Usage / decision:** The outcome need not be unexpected; compare eventually for something happening after time or delay. In the end gives the final outcome; eventually highlights time or delay. They overlap, but not in every context.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P098](#existing-p098).

**Replacement disposition:** [N067](cards.md#at-first--clause-eventuallyin-the-end--clause--over-time--clause--over-the-past--period--present-perfect)

**Sources:** STORY11.

<a id="story14"></a>

### STORY14 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Apparently, + clause`

**Use:** Report what seems true or what you have been told.

**Example:** Apparently, the store closed early.

**Usage / decision:** Apparently can signal appearances or secondhand information; it need not refer to a named speaker. According to names a source; apparently/from what I've heard may leave it unnamed. Apparent evidence and hearsay are not proof.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P086](#existing-p086).

**Replacement disposition:** [N005](cards.md#in-my-experience--clause--as-far-as-i-know--clause--from-what-i-have-heard--clause--apparently--clause)

**Sources:** STORY14.

<a id="story15"></a>

### STORY15 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `From what I've heard, + clause`

**Use:** Explain you're relying on secondhand information.

**Example:** From what I've heard, the interview is pretty tough.

**Usage / decision:** According to names a source; apparently/from what I've heard may leave it unnamed. Apparent evidence and hearsay are not proof.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P086](#existing-p086).

**Replacement disposition:** [N005](cards.md#in-my-experience--clause--as-far-as-i-know--clause--from-what-i-have-heard--clause--apparently--clause)

**Sources:** STORY15.

<a id="story18"></a>

### STORY18 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I couldn't believe + clause`

**Use:** Conclude a story with your reaction.

**Example:** I couldn't believe how expensive it was.

**Replacement disposition:** [N072](cards.md#luckilyfortunatelyunfortunately--clause--to-make-matters-worse--clause), [N073](cards.md#surprisingly--clause--i-could-not-believe--clause), [N074](cards.md#almostnearly--verb--almostnearly-allevery--noun)

**Sources:** STORY18.

## Amounts frequency and degree

<a id="amount01"></a>

### AMOUNT01 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `too + adjective + to + verb / adjective + enough + to + verb`

**Use:** Describe whether a degree is excessive or sufficient for an action.

**Example:** It's too heavy to carry.

**Usage / decision:** Degree relative to a possible action, not only numerical quantity. Existing bounded contrast.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P050](#existing-p050).

**Replacement disposition:** [N148](cards.md#too--adjectiveadverb--to--verb--adjectiveadverb--enough--to--verb--enough--noun--tofor)

**Sources:** AMOUNT01, AMOUNT02.

<a id="amount04"></a>

### AMOUNT04 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `usually / hardly ever + verb; every now and then, + clause / How often do you + verb?`

**Use:** Say an action almost never happens.

**Example:** I usually work from home. / I hardly ever commute. / Every now and then, I visit the office. / How often do you work there?

**Usage / decision:** A frequency family, not synonyms: usually = on most occasions; hardly ever = almost never; every now and then = occasionally. Show adverb placement, not a made-up numerical scale.

**Replacement disposition:** [N017](cards.md#usuallyoftenrarelyhardly-ever--main-verb--be--frequency-adverb--every-now-and-then--how-often-), [N018](cards.md#tend-to--verb--in-generalgenerallyin-most-cases--clause)

**Sources:** AMOUNT04, AMOUNT05, AMOUNT16, QUESTION03.

<a id="amount06"></a>

### AMOUNT06 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `X has increased by + amount`

**Use:** Compare amounts across two periods.

**Example:** Our costs have increased by ten percent.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P079](#existing-p079).

**Replacement disposition:** [N154](cards.md#increasedecrease-by--amount--increasedecrease-from-x-to-y--an-increasedecrease-in--variable), [N155](cards.md#account-formake-up--share--of--whole--fractionpercentage--of--group)

**Sources:** AMOUNT06.

<a id="amount07"></a>

### AMOUNT07 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `There was a decrease in + noun`

**Use:** Describe a reduction in a metric.

**Example:** There was a decrease in throughput.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P078](#existing-p078).

**Replacement disposition:** [N154](cards.md#increasedecrease-by--amount--increasedecrease-from-x-to-y--an-increasedecrease-in--variable), [N155](cards.md#account-formake-up--share--of--whole--fractionpercentage--of--group)

**Sources:** AMOUNT07.

<a id="amount09"></a>

### AMOUNT09 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `much/far/slightly/a little + comparative (+ than + comparison)`

**Use:** Emphasize the extent of a result.

**Example:** The interview was much harder than expected.

**Usage / decision:** One degree-of-difference family. More important and harder are both comparatives.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P053](#existing-p053).

**Replacement disposition:** [N144](cards.md#muchfarslightlya-little--comparative--than--anyno--comparative)

**Sources:** AMOUNT09, AMOUNT15.

<a id="amount10"></a>

### AMOUNT10 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `X is more or less Y`

**Use:** Say something is roughly accurate.

**Example:** That's more or less what happened.

**Replacement disposition:** [N158](cards.md#aboutroughly--number--more-or-less--quantitydescription--at-leastno-less-than--number--at-mostno-more-than--number)

**Sources:** AMOUNT10.

<a id="amount12"></a>

### AMOUNT12 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Roughly + number`

**Use:** State an approximate amount.

**Example:** The migration took roughly three hours.

**Replacement disposition:** [N158](cards.md#aboutroughly--number--more-or-less--quantitydescription--at-leastno-less-than--number--at-mostno-more-than--number)

**Sources:** AMOUNT12.

<a id="amount13"></a>

### AMOUNT13 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `No more than + number`

**Use:** Describe a comparison with a limit.

**Example:** The meeting should take no more than thirty minutes.

**Usage / decision:** Added coverage: Range, minimum, and maximum differ. No more than often minimizes; at most sets a maximum neutrally. Extend AMOUNT12/AMOUNT13 where appropriate.

**Added form (ADD46):** `between X and Y / from X to Y; at least / at most / up to + number`

It should take between two and three hours. / We need at least two reviewers. / The plan supports up to ten users.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C09-068](../connections/09-comparison-degree-result.md#c09-068), [C08-033](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-033), [C08-034](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-034).

**Replacement disposition:** [N158](cards.md#aboutroughly--number--more-or-less--quantitydescription--at-leastno-less-than--number--at-mostno-more-than--number)

**Sources:** AMOUNT13, ADD46.

<a id="amount14"></a>

### AMOUNT14 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `X accounts for + percentage + of Y`

**Use:** Express that one figure represents a share of a whole.

**Example:** Mobile traffic accounts for sixty percent of requests.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P077](#existing-p077).

**Replacement disposition:** [N154](cards.md#increasedecrease-by--amount--increasedecrease-from-x-to-y--an-increasedecrease-in--variable), [N155](cards.md#account-formake-up--share--of--whole--fractionpercentage--of--group)

**Sources:** AMOUNT14.

## Social small talk and everyday

<a id="social01"></a>

### SOCIAL01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `How have you been? / How's your day going? / What have you been up to? / How was + event?`

**Use:** Open a conversation by asking about well-being, activities, or an experience.

**Example:** How have you been? — Pretty good. / How's your day going? — Busy. / What have you been up to? — Mostly working. / How was your trip? — Great.

**Usage / decision:** A small-talk family: recent well-being, today's progress, recent activities, or a completed experience. Natural answers can overlap; don't teach them as four synonyms with an arbitrary hierarchy.

**Replacement disposition:** [N119](cards.md#how-have-you-been--how-is--eventday--going--how-is--task--coming-along--what-have-you-been-up-to--how-was--event)

**Sources:** SOCIAL01, SOCIAL02, SOCIAL03, SOCIAL07.

<a id="social04"></a>

### SOCIAL04 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `Do you have any plans for + time?`

**Use:** Ask about someone's plans.

**Example:** Do you have any plans for the weekend?

**Usage / decision:** Use PLAN07 for plans and an ordinary question form; do not create a duplicate just for the topic weekend.

**Original home / related coverage:** Catalog: [PLAN07](#plan07).

**Replacement disposition:** [N121](cards.md#what-do-you-think-ofabout--noun--how-do-you-feel-about--nounv-ing), [N112](cards.md#are-you-free--timeto--verb--does--time--work-for-you--could-we-move--event--to--time--i-cant-make-it-to--event--can-we-make-it--time--when-will-you-be-ready-to--verb--do-you-have-any-plans-for--time)

**Sources:** SOCIAL04.

<a id="social05"></a>

### SOCIAL05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `(Do you) want to + verb? / We should + shared activity + sometime`

**Use:** Ask casually whether someone wants to spend time together.

**Example:** Want to grab coffee? / We should get together sometime.

**Usage / decision:** A direct invitation versus suggesting a future get-together. Should here can be a friendly suggestion, not an obligation.

**Replacement disposition:** [N113](cards.md#do-you-want-towould-you-like-to--verb--how-about--nounv-ing--why-dont-we--verb--lets--verb)

**Sources:** SOCIAL05, SOCIAL17.

<a id="social06"></a>

### SOCIAL06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `What do you think of/about + noun? / How do you feel about + noun or V-ing?`

**Use:** Ask for an evaluation, attitude, or feeling about a topic.

**Example:** What do you think about working remotely? / How do you feel about working remotely?

**Usage / decision:** Both invite a view. Think often invites an evaluation; feel can highlight attitude or willingness. Either is natural for many proposals.

**Replacement disposition:** [N121](cards.md#what-do-you-think-ofabout--noun--how-do-you-feel-about--nounv-ing), [N112](cards.md#are-you-free--timeto--verb--does--time--work-for-you--could-we-move--event--to--time--i-cant-make-it-to--event--can-we-make-it--time--when-will-you-be-ready-to--verb--do-you-have-any-plans-for--time)

**Sources:** SOCIAL06, QUESTION11.

<a id="social08"></a>

### SOCIAL08 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `I noticed that + clause`

**Use:** Introduce an interesting thing you recently noticed.

**Example:** I noticed that the trains are less crowded lately.

**Usage / decision:** Existing vocabulary home: notice / find out; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `notice / find out` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** SOCIAL08.

<a id="social09"></a>

### SOCIAL09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I've been wondering + wh-clause`

**Use:** Share something you've been curious about.

**Example:** I've been wondering why the rent is so high.

**Replacement disposition:** [N169](cards.md#do-you-knowcould-you-tell-me--wh-word--subject--verb--i-wonder--wh-clauseifwhether-clause--do-you-happen-to-know--noun)

**Sources:** SOCIAL09.

<a id="social10"></a>

### SOCIAL10 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `I just found out that + clause`

**Use:** Share something you recently learned.

**Example:** I just found out that the store is closing.

**Usage / decision:** Existing vocabulary home: notice / find out; check its actual sense/pattern before expanding.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `notice / find out` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** SOCIAL10.

<a id="social12"></a>

**Pattern:** `That reminds me of + noun`

**Use:** Say an experience reminds you of another.

**Example:** That reminds me of our last trip.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

<a id="social13"></a>

**Pattern:** `Do you happen to know + person?`

**Use:** Ask if a person recognizes someone.

**Example:** Do you happen to know anyone who works there?

**Replacement disposition:** [N169](cards.md#do-you-knowcould-you-tell-me--wh-word--subject--verb--i-wonder--wh-clauseifwhether-clause--do-you-happen-to-know--noun)

### SOCIAL14 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `Are you familiar with + noun?`

**Use:** Ask whether someone is familiar with something.

**Example:** Are you familiar with this neighborhood?

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

<a id="social15"></a>

### SOCIAL15 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `be (not really) into + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Say you're interested in an activity.

**Example:** I'm really into hiking lately.

**Usage / decision:** Casual interest or enthusiasm. Negation belongs on the same lexical note. Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Pattern:** `It was great talking to you`

**Use:** End a conversation politely.

**Example:** It was great talking to you. Have a good one!

**Added form (ADD13):** `I should get going / I need to get back to + task / Can we talk later?`

I should get going, but it was great seeing you. / I need to get back to work. Can we talk later?

**Replacement disposition:** [N127](cards.md#it-was-great-talking-to-you--i-should-get-going--lets-keep-in-touch)

**Sources:** SOCIAL18, ADD13.

## Learning growth and self expression

<a id="learn01"></a>

### LEARN01 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `I can't remember how to + verb`

**Use:** Express difficulty remembering an expression.

**Example:** I can't remember how to pronounce this word.

**Usage / decision:** The current wh-word + to-infinitive grammar card owns this complement; no duplicate omission or infinitive card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P109](#existing-p109).

**Replacement disposition:** [N251](cards.md#for--person--to--verb--wh-word--to--verb)

**Sources:** LEARN01.

<a id="learn02"></a>

### LEARN02 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `What I struggle with most is + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Explain your biggest difficulty.

**Example:** What I struggle with most is speaking spontaneously.

**Usage / decision:** Add What I struggle with most; preserve with + noun/V-ing for struggle.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P060](#existing-p060).

**Replacement disposition:** [N034](cards.md#what-i-needvaluelike-most-is--nounclause--what-matters-most-is--nounclause--what-i-struggle-with-most-is--nounv-ing)

**Sources:** LEARN02.

**Pattern:** `I'm getting better at + V-ing`

**Use:** Say something has become easier with practice.

**Example:** I'm getting better at explaining technical ideas.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** LEARN03.

<a id="learn04"></a>

### LEARN04 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `I need more practice with + noun`

**Use:** Say you need time to practice something.

**Example:** I need more practice with American vowels.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** LEARN04.

<a id="learn05"></a>

### LEARN05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Can you give me an example of + noun?`

**Use:** Ask for a realistic example.

**Example:** Can you give me an example of that pattern?

**Replacement disposition:** [N223](cards.md#can-you-give-me-an-example-of--noun--could-you-explain--noun--in-plain-english--i-do-not-understand-whyhow--clause)

**Sources:** LEARN05.

<a id="learn06"></a>

### LEARN06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Could you explain it in plain English?`

**Use:** Ask for a simpler explanation.

**Example:** Could you explain it in plain English?

**Replacement disposition:** [N223](cards.md#can-you-give-me-an-example-of--noun--could-you-explain--noun--in-plain-english--i-do-not-understand-whyhow--clause)

**Sources:** LEARN06.

<a id="learn07"></a>

### LEARN07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `How would you say + noun or quoted phrase + in English?`

**Use:** Ask how to express an idea.

**Example:** How would you say this in everyday English?

**Replacement disposition:** [N224](cards.md#how-would-you-say-x-in-english--does-x-sound-natural--can-i-use-x-to-mean-y--what-is-the-difference-between-x-and-y)

**Sources:** LEARN07.

<a id="learn08"></a>

### LEARN08 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I don't understand why + clause`

**Use:** Explain why an example is confusing.

**Example:** I don't understand why we need 'to' here.

**Replacement disposition:** [N223](cards.md#can-you-give-me-an-example-of--noun--could-you-explain--noun--in-plain-english--i-do-not-understand-whyhow--clause)

**Sources:** LEARN08.

<a id="learn09"></a>

### LEARN09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `What's the difference between X and Y? / How does X compare with/to Y? / Can you compare X with/to Y?`

**Use:** Request a comparison or an explanation of differences.

**Example:** What's the difference between 'reply' and 'respond'?

**Usage / decision:** One comparison-request family. Difference targets distinctions; compare can include similarities as well. With and to overlap here.

**Replacement disposition:** [N224](cards.md#how-would-you-say-x-in-english--does-x-sound-natural--can-i-use-x-to-mean-y--what-is-the-difference-between-x-and-y)

**Sources:** LEARN09, LEARN15, QUESTION05.

<a id="learn10"></a>

### LEARN10 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Does this sound natural?`

**Use:** Check whether a sentence is natural.

**Example:** Does this sound natural in American English?

**Replacement disposition:** [N224](cards.md#how-would-you-say-x-in-english--does-x-sound-natural--can-i-use-x-to-mean-y--what-is-the-difference-between-x-and-y)

**Sources:** LEARN10.

<a id="learn11"></a>

### LEARN11 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Can I use X to mean Y?`

**Use:** Ask whether wording fits an intended meaning.

**Example:** Can I use 'migrate' to mean switching providers?

**Replacement disposition:** [N224](cards.md#how-would-you-say-x-in-english--does-x-sound-natural--can-i-use-x-to-mean-y--what-is-the-difference-between-x-and-y)

**Sources:** LEARN11.

<a id="learn13"></a>

### LEARN13 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `I learned X by + V-ing`

**Use:** Explain how you learned something.

**Example:** I learned that expression by watching interviews.

**Usage / decision:** The construction is already taught. Keep the example for transfer practice; no automatic standalone production card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P041](#existing-p041).

**Replacement disposition:** [N206](cards.md#preposition--subject--v-ing)

**Sources:** LEARN13.

<a id="learn14"></a>

### LEARN14 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `This helped me realize that + clause`

**Use:** Say an exercise reveals what you don't know.

**Example:** This helped me realize that I need more practice.

**Usage / decision:** Use the existing help + object + (to) + verb construction; realize is the example action.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024); Connection evidence: [C02-111](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-111).

**Replacement disposition:** [N193](cards.md#help--person--to--verb), [N194](cards.md#preventstopkeep--person--from--v-ing)

**Sources:** LEARN14.

## Useful verb and noun patterns

<a id="verb01"></a>

### VERB01 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `make/let/have + object + base verb`

**Use:** Describe causing, permitting, or arranging another person's action.

**Example:** Let me take a look.

**Usage / decision:** Reuse the existing family: cause/force, permit, arrange/instruct. The shared shape does not make the verbs synonyms.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P025](#existing-p025).

**Replacement disposition:** [N192](cards.md#makelethave--person--base-verb--haveget--object--past-participle--wantneed--object--past-participle--get--person--to--verb--be-made-to--verb)

**Sources:** VERB01, VERB02.

<a id="verb04"></a>

### VERB04 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `Help + person + (to) + verb`

**Use:** Explain that something helped you achieve a task.

**Example:** This tool helped me finish faster.

**Usage / decision:** The construction is already taught. Keep the example for transfer practice; no automatic standalone production card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024); Connection evidence: [C02-111](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-111).

**Replacement disposition:** [N193](cards.md#help--person--to--verb), [N194](cards.md#preventstopkeep--person--from--v-ing)

**Sources:** VERB04.

<a id="verb05"></a>

### VERB05 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `Advise + person + to + verb`

**Use:** Describe giving someone advice about an action.

**Example:** The doctor advised me to rest.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024).

**Replacement disposition:** [N191](cards.md#verb--object--to--verb)

**Sources:** VERB05.

<a id="verb06"></a>

### VERB06 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `Ask + person + to + verb`

**Use:** Describe asking someone to take action.

**Example:** I asked him to send me the file.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024).

**Replacement disposition:** [N191](cards.md#verb--object--to--verb)

**Sources:** VERB06.

<a id="verb07"></a>

### VERB07 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `Tell + person + to + verb`

**Use:** Describe telling someone to take action.

**Example:** She told me to wait outside.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024).

**Replacement disposition:** [N191](cards.md#verb--object--to--verb)

**Sources:** VERB07.

<a id="verb08"></a>

### VERB08 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `Expect + person or thing + to + verb`

**Use:** Express an expectation about a result.

**Example:** I expect the test to finish soon.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024).

**Replacement disposition:** [N191](cards.md#verb--object--to--verb)

**Sources:** VERB08.

<a id="verb09"></a>

### VERB09 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Convince + person + to + verb`

**Use:** Describe persuading someone to change behavior.

**Example:** We convinced the team to postpone the launch.

**Usage / decision:** Add convince to the existing object + to-infinitive family if useful; do not create a separate syntax card for this verb. Persuade and convince overlap for getting someone to act.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024).

**Replacement disposition:** [N191](cards.md#verb--object--to--verb)

**Sources:** VERB09.

<a id="verb10"></a>

### VERB10 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `Encourage + person + to + verb`

**Use:** Describe encouraging someone to try something.

**Example:** My manager encouraged me to apply.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024).

**Replacement disposition:** [N191](cards.md#verb--object--to--verb)

**Sources:** VERB10.

<a id="verb13"></a>

### VERB13 · REUSE

**Pattern:** `suggest/recommend + V-ing / (that) + subject + base verb`

**Use:** Describe something you recommend trying.

**Example:** I'd recommend checking the logs first.

**Usage / decision:** Already one card with both verbs and both common complement shapes.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P033](#existing-p033).

**Replacement disposition:** [N205](cards.md#suggestrecommend--v-ing--suggestrecommend-that--subject--base-verb--it-is-importantessential-that--subject--base-verb)

**Sources:** VERB13, VERB14.

<a id="verb15"></a>

### VERB15 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `Decline + noun or to + verb`

**Use:** Describe rejecting a proposal without accepting it.

**Example:** I declined the invitation.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Sources:** VERB15.

<a id="verb16"></a>

**Pattern:** `Require + noun or V-ing`

**Use:** Say something requires a specific task.

**Example:** The position requires working with international teams.

Require + object + to + verb also occurs: The policy requires users to sign in. The V-ing example names what a job entails.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

**Pattern:** `Explain + thing + to + person`

<a id="verb18"></a>

**Pattern:** `Discuss + subject + with + person`

**Use:** Describe discussing a subject with a person.

**Example:** Let's discuss the design with the team.

**Usage / decision:** The construction is already taught. Keep the example for transfer practice; no automatic standalone production card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-002](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-002).

**Replacement disposition:** [N188](cards.md#verb--direct-object--verb--object--preposition-phrase--givesend--person--thing--thing--to--person)

<a id="verb19"></a>

### VERB19 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `Gain experience in + field or V-ing`

**Use:** Describe obtaining experience in a field.

**Example:** I'm trying to gain experience in system design.

**Usage / decision:** Lexical candidate only; no matching headword established in the current vocabulary master. Preview there if needed rather than automatically duplicating it here. This collocation is distinct from restoring the previously removed standalone experience/practice/expertise vocabulary group.

**Replacement disposition:** Lexical expression; no separate Patterns card under the learner’s known-vocabulary assumption. Existing vocabulary sources and the catalog retain its usage reference.

<a id="verb20"></a>

**Pattern:** `Apply for + position or opportunity`

**Use:** Describe applying for an opportunity.

## Modals ability and regret

<a id="modal01"></a>

### MODAL01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `may/might/could + base verb`

**Use:** Say something might be possible.

**Example:** It may / might / could take longer than expected.

**Usage / decision:** All can express an uncertain possibility. Could also expresses ability or a conditional option in other contexts. Do not assign fixed probabilities.

**Replacement disposition:** [N014](cards.md#maymightcould--base-verb--there-is-a-chancepossibility-that--clause--be-likelyunlikely-to--verb--be-bound-to--verb)

**Sources:** MODAL01, MODAL02.

<a id="modal03"></a>

### MODAL03 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `You should + verb / You might want to + verb`

**Use:** Give advice directly or with a softer introduction.

**Example:** You should save a backup. / You might want to save a backup.

**Usage / decision:** Both give advice. Might want to often softens it but can still sound pointed in context; should is more direct.

**Replacement disposition:** [N088](cards.md#should--verb--might-want-to--verb--had-better--verb--would-be-better-off--v-ing--have-you-tried--v-ing)

**Sources:** MODAL03, MODAL04.

<a id="modal05"></a>

### MODAL05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `have to / need to + verb; don't have to / don't need to + verb; can/can't + verb`

**Use:** Express an obligation.

**Example:** You have to register. / You don't have to attend. / You can attend remotely. / You can't record the session.

**Usage / decision:** A review family, not one giant card: obligation, no obligation, and permission/prohibition. No obligation does not mean prohibition. Could split into two small contrasts after preview. Must not also expresses prohibition; be allowed to expresses permission and not be allowed to its absence. Don't have to means the action is optional, not forbidden.

**Replacement disposition:** [N087](cards.md#have-tohave-got-toneed-tomust--verb--do-not-have-todo-not-need-to--verb--did-not-have-to--verb--be-going-to-have-to--verb--cannotmust-not--verb--be-supposed-to--verb--be-supposed-to-be--adjectivenoun)

**Sources:** MODAL05, MODAL06, MODAL07, MODAL08, MODAL22.

<a id="modal09"></a>

### MODAL09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I can finally + verb`

**Use:** Express an ability gained recently.

**Example:** I can finally understand that accent.

**Replacement disposition:** [N096](cards.md#canbe-able-to--verb--could--verb--managed-to--verb--could-not--verb--in-time--cannot-seem-to--verb)

**Sources:** MODAL09.

<a id="modal10"></a>

### MODAL10 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I can't seem to + verb`

**Use:** Explain that you're unable to do something for now.

**Example:** I can't seem to connect to the Wi-Fi.

**Replacement disposition:** [N096](cards.md#canbe-able-to--verb--could--verb--managed-to--verb--could-not--verb--in-time--cannot-seem-to--verb)

**Sources:** MODAL10.

<a id="modal11"></a>

### MODAL11 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I could have + past participle`

**Use:** Express an opportunity you missed in the past.

**Example:** I could have taken an earlier train.

**Replacement disposition:** [N084](cards.md#could-have--past-participle--shouldshould-not-have--past-participle)

**Sources:** MODAL11.

<a id="modal12"></a>

### MODAL12 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `should/shouldn't have + past participle`

**Use:** Describe a past action you now regret.

**Example:** I shouldn't have stayed up so late.

**Usage / decision:** Past advice or regret: something should have happened, or should not have happened. Keep apart from inference such as should have arrived by now.

**Replacement disposition:** [N084](cards.md#could-have--past-participle--shouldshould-not-have--past-participle)

**Sources:** MODAL12, MODAL13.

<a id="modal14"></a>

### MODAL14 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `must/might/couldn't + base verb / must/might/couldn't have + past participle`

**Use:** Make a strong deduction about a past event.

**Example:** That must be the wrong address. / He must have forgotten. / I might have left my keys at work. / She couldn't have known.

**Usage / decision:** Evidence-based conclusions or possibilities about now versus the past. Could not have can also mean lack of opportunity/ability; context decides. Use two small time-based cards if a combined Front is overloaded.

**Replacement disposition:** [N085](cards.md#mustmightcannot--base-verb--mustmightcould-not-have--past-participle--should--base-verb)

**Sources:** MODAL14, MODAL15, MODAL16, MODAL21.

<a id="modal17"></a>

### MODAL17 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `was/were going to + verb, but + clause / meant to + verb, but + clause`

**Use:** Describe what you intended to do in the past.

**Example:** I was going to call you, but I got distracted.

**Usage / decision:** Both can explain an unrealized intention; was going to can also describe a prediction. The but clause shows what changed.

**Replacement disposition:** [N060](cards.md#be-about-to--verb--when--event--waswere-going-to--verb-but--clause--meant-to--verb-but--clause)

**Sources:** MODAL17, MODAL18.

<a id="modal20"></a>

### MODAL20 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `X should + base verb + soon`

**Use:** Express a reasonably certain guess.

**Example:** The train should arrive soon.

**Replacement disposition:** [N085](cards.md#mustmightcannot--base-verb--mustmightcould-not-have--past-participle--should--base-verb)

**Sources:** MODAL20.

## Question frames and answers

<a id="question01"></a>

### QUESTION01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `How come + statement word order?`

**Use:** Ask for the cause of something informally.

**Example:** How come you're still awake?

**Replacement disposition:** [N168](cards.md#how-come--subject--verb--why--auxiliary--subject--verb)

**Sources:** QUESTION01.

<a id="question06"></a>

### QUESTION06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Why did you decide to + verb?`

**Use:** Ask for reasons for a choice.

**Example:** Why did you decide to switch jobs?

**Replacement disposition:** [N167](cards.md#dodoesdid--subject--base-verb--beauxiliarymodal--subject---whowhat--verb-)

**Sources:** QUESTION06.

<a id="question07"></a>

### QUESTION07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `What happened to + noun?`

**Use:** Ask what happened to something.

**Example:** What happened to your old laptop?

**Replacement disposition:** [N167](cards.md#dodoesdid--subject--base-verb--beauxiliarymodal--subject---whowhat--verb-)

**Sources:** QUESTION07.

<a id="question09"></a>

### QUESTION09 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `When will you be ready to + verb?`

**Use:** Ask when someone will be ready.

**Example:** When will you be ready to leave?

**Replacement disposition:** [N112](cards.md#are-you-free--timeto--verb--does--time--work-for-you--could-we-move--event--to--time--i-cant-make-it-to--event--can-we-make-it--time--when-will-you-be-ready-to--verb--do-you-have-any-plans-for--time)

**Sources:** QUESTION09.

<a id="question10"></a>

### QUESTION10 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `How does X work?`

**Use:** Ask someone to describe a process.

**Example:** How does the new payment system work?

**Replacement disposition:** [N167](cards.md#dodoesdid--subject--base-verb--beauxiliarymodal--subject---whowhat--verb-)

**Sources:** QUESTION10.

<a id="question12"></a>

### QUESTION12 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `What does X look like? / How does X look?`

**Use:** Ask about the appearance of something.

**Example:** What does the new office look like?

**Usage / decision:** Often description versus evaluation, but overlapping answers are possible. What does the office look like? asks for a description; How does this jacket look? invites an assessment.

**Replacement disposition:** [N006](cards.md#it-seemsappears-that--clause--it-seemslookssoundsfeels-like--clause), [N007](cards.md#looksoundfeeltastesmell--adjective--looksoundfeeltastesmell-like--noun), [N008](cards.md#what-does-x-look-like--how-does-x-look)

**Sources:** QUESTION12, QUESTION13.

<a id="question18"></a>

### QUESTION18 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `What would you like me to + verb?`

**Use:** Ask what a person wants you to do.

**Example:** What would you like me to focus on?

**Replacement disposition:** [N111](cards.md#do-you-want-me-to--verb--do-you-need-a-hand-with--noun--what-would-you-like-me-to--verb)

**Sources:** QUESTION18.

<a id="question19"></a>

### QUESTION19 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `What are our options for + noun or V-ing?`

**Use:** Ask about available alternatives.

**Example:** What are our options for getting home?

**Replacement disposition:** [N093](cards.md#either-one-works-for-me--what-are-our-options-for--nounv-ing--which-would-you-recommend), [N094](cards.md#be-willingunwilling-to--verb)

**Sources:** QUESTION19.

<a id="question20"></a>

### QUESTION20 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `When was the last time you + past verb?`

**Use:** Ask when a person last did something.

**Example:** When was the last time you visited your parents?

**Replacement disposition:** [N055](cards.md#the-firstlast-time--past-clause--clause--this-is-the-first-time--present-perfect--when-was-the-last-time--past-clause)

**Sources:** QUESTION20.
