## Additional coverage found in review

<a id="add01"></a>

### ADD01 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'm + name/role; I work as + role / for + employer / on + project`

**Use:** Introduce yourself and your work.

**Example:** I'm Lewis, a software engineer. / I work for a software company, mainly on developer tools.

**Usage / decision:** As names a role, for an employer/client, and on a task or project. Reuse WORK03 for working on; this family adds a useful introduction.

**Replacement disposition:** [N128](cards.md#i-work-asforon--noun--i-have--duration--of-experience-inwith--noun--my-role-involvesinvolved--v-ing)

**Sources:** ADD01.

<a id="add02"></a>

### ADD02 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I have + duration + of experience in/with + field/tool; My role involved + V-ing`

**Use:** Describe relevant experience in an interview.

**Example:** I have three years of experience with Python. / My role involved maintaining the deployment pipeline.

**Usage / decision:** Experience with a tool, in a field, and doing a task can overlap. Pair experience with a concrete contribution; do not make one card for every profession.

**Replacement disposition:** [N128](cards.md#i-work-asforon--noun--i-have--duration--of-experience-inwith--noun--my-role-involvesinvolved--v-ing)

**Sources:** ADD02.

<a id="add03"></a>

### ADD03 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `I was responsible for + V-ing; I + action, which + result`

**Use:** Explain a contribution and its outcome.

**Example:** I was responsible for automating releases. / I added validation, which reduced configuration errors.

**Usage / decision:** Reuse responsibility; add a contribution/result example only where the current cards do not make that interview function available.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P075](#existing-p075).

**Replacement disposition:** [N129](cards.md#be-responsible-for--nounv-ing--my-contribution-was-to--verb--i--action-which--result)

**Sources:** ADD03.

<a id="add04"></a>

### ADD04 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Can/Could I get + noun? / I'd like + noun, please`

**Use:** Order or ask for an item.

**Example:** Could I get a coffee, please? / I'd like a receipt, please.

**Usage / decision:** Both are ordinary service requests. Existing without-ingredient card covers a narrower customization task; expand it or keep this as one general request frame, not one card per venue.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P128](#existing-p128).

**Replacement disposition:** [N225](cards.md#cancould-i-get--noun--i-would-like--noun-please--without--ingredient--what-does-x-come-with)

**Sources:** ADD04.

<a id="add05"></a>

### ADD05 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I ordered/asked for X, but I got Y. Could you + remedy?`

**Use:** Describe a purchase or request that was wrong.

**Example:** I ordered the small size, but I got a large. Could you exchange it? / I asked for a receipt, but I didn't get one. Could you print it?

**Usage / decision:** State the mismatch and the requested remedy. Reuse the existing missing-item reminder as related coverage, not as proof this whole task is already taught.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P131](#existing-p131).

**Replacement disposition:** [N226](cards.md#i-orderedasked-for-x-but-i-got-y--i-have-not-gotten-my--item--yet--could-you--remedy)

**Sources:** ADD05.

<a id="add06"></a>

### ADD06 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Do you have + noun + in + size/color? / Is there another + option/time?`

**Use:** Ask about availability or alternatives.

**Example:** Do you have this in a smaller size? / Is there another appointment time?

**Usage / decision:** Availability requests transfer across stores, bookings, and workplace scheduling; avoid a separate note for every object.

**Replacement disposition:** [N227](cards.md#do-you-have--item--in--sizecolor--is-there-another--optiontime), [N228](cards.md#i-would-like-to-bookreschedulecancel--noun)

**Sources:** ADD06.

<a id="add07"></a>

### ADD07 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'd like to + action (book/reschedule/cancel + appointment/reservation)`

**Use:** Reserve, change, or cancel an arrangement.

**Example:** I'd like to reschedule my appointment. / I'd like to book a table for two.

**Usage / decision:** Same service-request construction with different actions. The verbs are not synonyms; a short example set is sufficient.

**Replacement disposition:** [N227](cards.md#do-you-have--item--in--sizecolor--is-there-another--optiontime), [N228](cards.md#i-would-like-to-bookreschedulecancel--noun)

**Sources:** ADD07.

<a id="add11"></a>

### ADD11 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I'd rather not + verb / Please don't + verb / I need you to + verb`

**Use:** Set a clear boundary.

**Example:** I'd rather not discuss my salary. / Please don't share that file. / I need you to ask before changing the settings.

**Usage / decision:** Compare soft preference, direct request to stop, and an explicit need. FEEL14 already expresses discomfort; P024 owns need + object + to.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P024](#existing-p024), [P083](#existing-p083).

**Replacement disposition:** [N115](cards.md#that-would-be-great-thanks--im-good-thanks--thanks-for-asking-but--clause--id-love-to-but--clause--i-would-rather-not--verb), [N116](cards.md#please-do-not--verb--i-need-you-to--verb--i-need--noun--from--person)

**Sources:** ADD11.

<a id="add17"></a>

### ADD17 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `Just a heads-up: + clause / Could you + verb + by + deadline?`

**Use:** Distinguish an update, a request, and a deadline.

**Example:** Just a heads-up: I'll be offline tomorrow. / Could you review this by Thursday?

**Usage / decision:** The first informs, the second asks for action. Avoid implying that a heads-up alone creates a commitment.

**Replacement disposition:** [N132](cards.md#we-are-blocked-by--noun--we-need-more-time-to--verb), [N133](cards.md#just-a-heads-up--clause--i-just-wanted-to-let-you-knowcheckask--clause--just-a-quick-question--question), [N131](cards.md#here-is-what-we-agreed-on--the-next-step-is-to--verb)

**Sources:** ADD17.

<a id="add18"></a>

### ADD18 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `I can + verb + by + time, as long as + condition / I can't promise X, but I can Y`

**Use:** Express commitment with a limit.

**Example:** I can finish by Friday, as long as the API is ready. / I can't promise a fix today, but I can investigate.

**Usage / decision:** Compose TIME18 and P044; include as practice rather than another note for every combination.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P044](#existing-p044).

**Replacement disposition:** [N075](cards.md#if--present--presentwillcanmay--as-long-asprovided-that--clause--only-if--clause)

**Sources:** ADD18.

<a id="add19"></a>

### ADD19 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I've had + symptom + for + duration / It hurts when + clause / I need help`

**Use:** Report symptoms, duration, or an immediate need.

**Example:** I've had a headache since yesterday. / It hurts when I move my arm. / I need help. My friend has collapsed.

**Usage / decision:** Language for describing a situation and requesting help, not diagnosis or treatment. Reuse duration grammar; medical vocabulary remains separate.

**Replacement disposition:** [N232](cards.md#i-have-had--symptom--for--duration--it-hurts-when--clause), [N233](cards.md#i-need-help-with--noun--could-you-call--personservice)

**Sources:** ADD19.

<a id="add20"></a>

### ADD20 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `can/can't afford + noun / to + verb`

**Use:** Describe affordability.

**Example:** I can't afford a new laptop right now. / We can't afford to delay the release.

**Usage / decision:** Money or tolerable cost/consequences. Existing connection note owns the complement; use as a vocabulary pattern unless a dedicated production need remains.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-087](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-087).

**Replacement disposition:** [N274](cards.md#have-access-to--noun), [N275](cards.md#cancannot-afford--nounto--verb), [N276](cards.md#work-well-for--personpurpose)

**Sources:** ADD20.

<a id="add23"></a>

### ADD23 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `For example/instance, + clause / In other words, + clause / In fact, + clause`

**Use:** Distinguish illustration, clarification, and correction.

**Example:** Some checks are cheap. For example, we can validate the schema. / It runs locally; in other words, it doesn't need a server. / It isn't slower. In fact, it's faster.

**Usage / decision:** Reuse P064 and P065; add in fact for emphasis or correction, not as a synonym for in other words. This is a cross-reference comparison, not a new omnibus card.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P064](#existing-p064), [P065](#existing-p065).

**Replacement disposition:** [N037](cards.md#for-examplefor-instance--clause--such-aslike--nounv-ing), [N038](cards.md#in-factactually--clause)

**Sources:** ADD23.

<a id="add25"></a>

### ADD25 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `I hope + clause / I wish + past clause / I wish + had + past participle`

**Use:** Report facts versus imagined wishes.

**Example:** I hope I can attend. / I wish I had more time. / I wish I'd applied earlier.

**Usage / decision:** Hope concerns a desired possibility; wish + past describes an unreal present, and wish + past perfect an unreal past. I wish you would reply concerns a desired change, not an ordinary future prediction. Split present/past if needed; not all four on an overloaded note. Reference: [Cambridge on wish](https://dictionary.cambridge.org/us/grammar/british-grammar/wish%3A).

**Replacement disposition:** [N083](cards.md#i-hope--clause--i-wish--past-form--i-wish--had--past-participle--i-wish--would--verb)

**Sources:** ADD25.

<a id="add26"></a>

### ADD26 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `If + had + past participle, would + base verb + now`

**Use:** Connect a past counterfactual to a present result.

**Example:** If I'd accepted that job, I'd live in Seattle now. / If we'd added monitoring, we'd know what happened.

**Usage / decision:** A past condition with a present consequence. Keep alongside COND05; do not force would have when the result is now.

**Replacement disposition:** [N082](cards.md#if--had--past-participle--wouldcould-have--past-participle--if--had--past-participle--would--base-verb--now)

**Sources:** ADD26.

<a id="add28"></a>

### ADD28 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `whether or not + clause / no matter what/how + clause`

**Use:** Express indifference to which condition holds.

**Example:** I'll go whether or not it rains. / No matter how busy I am, I take a break.

**Usage / decision:** Reuse the existing whether/or-not card; add no matter + wh-clause if it helps production. Do not merge it into the neutral question I'm not sure whether.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P048](#existing-p048).

**Replacement disposition:** [N077](cards.md#even-though--fact--clause--even-if--possible-case--clause--whether-or-notno-matter-whathow--clause--it-doesnt-matter--wh-if-clause)

**Sources:** ADD28.

<a id="add30"></a>

### ADD30 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `will be + V-ing + at/when + time`

**Use:** Describe activity at a future time.

**Example:** I'll be traveling at that time. / When you arrive, I'll be working from home.

**Usage / decision:** Future activity or an expected course of events. This fills a tense-use gap; future completion already has P097.

**Replacement disposition:** [N065](cards.md#will--base-verb--be-going-to--base-verb--present-continuous--future-time--will-be--v-ing--simple-present-for-a-timetable)

**Sources:** ADD30.

<a id="add32"></a>

### ADD32 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `simple present / am/is/are + V-ing`

**Use:** Describe ordinary habits versus current temporary activity.

**Example:** I work remotely. / I'm working from a hotel this week.

**Usage / decision:** General practice versus current activity/state in progress. Some verbs allow both with different meanings; avoid a blanket rule that every present state must be continuous.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P001](#existing-p001).

**Replacement disposition:** [N048](cards.md#simple-present--amisare--v-ing--dodoes--subject--base-verb--amisare--subject--v-ing)

**Sources:** ADD32.

<a id="add33"></a>

### ADD33 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `be/get + past participle (+ by + agent)`

**Use:** Name the recipient of an action.

**Example:** The release was delayed. / My bag got damaged during the flight.

**Usage / decision:** Existing P092/P093 teach narrower aspect contrasts; this is the base passive perspective. Get is conversational and often highlights an event affecting the subject; it cannot replace be in every passive/state.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P092](#existing-p092), [P093](#existing-p093).

**Replacement disposition:** [N209](cards.md#beget--past-participle--be-being--past-participle--havehas-been--past-participle--modal--be--past-participle)

**Sources:** ADD33.

<a id="add34"></a>

### ADD34 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `say (that) + clause / tell + person + clause / ask + person + if/whether/wh-clause`

**Use:** Report what someone said or asked.

**Example:** She said she was busy. / She told me she was busy. / She asked whether I was available.

**Usage / decision:** Tell needs the recipient in this reporting sense. Embedded questions use statement word order. Backshift depends on reporting viewpoint and continuing relevance, not a rule to change every tense.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-120](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-120), [C02-121](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-121).

**Replacement disposition:** [N211](cards.md#say-that--clause--tell--person--clause--ask--person--ifwhetherwh-clause), [N212](cards.md#it-is-saidreportedbelievedexpected-that--clause--subject--be-saidreportedbelievedexpected--to-infinitive)

**Sources:** ADD34.

<a id="add35"></a>

### ADD35 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `Do you know / Could you tell me + wh-word + subject + verb?`

**Use:** Ask an indirect question.

**Example:** Do you know when the store closes? / Could you tell me where the meeting is?

**Usage / decision:** Reuse the direction-question overview and clarification card; add a transferable non-location example. No second question inversion inside the embedded clause.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P101](#existing-p101), [P125](#existing-p125).

**Replacement disposition:** [N169](cards.md#do-you-knowcould-you-tell-me--wh-word--subject--verb--i-wonder--wh-clauseifwhether-clause--do-you-happen-to-know--noun)

**Sources:** ADD35.

<a id="add37"></a>

### ADD37 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `positive clause, negative auxiliary + pronoun? / negative clause, positive auxiliary + pronoun?`

**Use:** Confirm a belief with a question tag.

**Example:** It's ready, isn't it? / You haven't sent it, have you?

**Usage / decision:** Match tense, auxiliary, and pronoun. Intonation changes whether this seeks information or agreement. Conversational right? is another check, not the same grammar.

**Replacement disposition:** [N170](cards.md#positive-statement-negative-auxiliary--pronoun--negative-statement-positive-auxiliary--pronoun)

**Sources:** ADD37.

<a id="add38"></a>

### ADD38 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `Yes/No + subject + auxiliary / So + auxiliary + subject / Neither + auxiliary + subject`

**Use:** Give short answers and show shared experience.

**Example:** Have you tried it? — Yes, I have. / I work remotely. — So do I. / I haven't tried it. — Neither have I.

**Usage / decision:** Existing ellipsis card P110 owns omitted repeated actions. Check its full specification before adding So/Neither examples there; Me too/Me neither are everyday alternatives. No new omission card authorized by this catalog.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P110](#existing-p110).

**Replacement disposition:** [N252](cards.md#doauxiliarymodalto--repeated-action)

**Sources:** ADD38.

<a id="add39"></a>

### ADD39 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `There is/are + noun / There are + plural noun + V-ing`

**Use:** Introduce existence and describe a developing group.

**Example:** There's a problem with the login page. / There are more people working remotely now.

**Usage / decision:** Use be once in the basic there are + noun + V-ing frame. People who are working is also valid. P080 covers a growing number; it does not itself teach this existential frame.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P080](#existing-p080).

**Replacement disposition:** [N172](cards.md#there-isare--noun--there-isare--noun--v-ing--there-seems-to-be--noun--there-used-to-be--noun)

**Sources:** ADD39.

<a id="add40"></a>

### ADD40 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `few/a few + plural count noun / little/a little + uncountable noun`

**Use:** Distinguish too little from a small positive amount.

**Example:** We have few options left. / We have a few options left. / We have little time. / We have a little time.

**Usage / decision:** Few/little emphasize shortage; a few/a little indicate some. Keep countability explicit; this is a useful small contrast, not a list of all determiners.

**Replacement disposition:** [N159](cards.md#fewa-few--plural-count-noun--a-couple-of--plural-count-noun--littlea-little--uncountable-noun--fewerless--noun)

**Sources:** ADD40.

<a id="add41"></a>

### ADD41 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `all/most/some of + determiner + noun; both/either/neither + noun`

**Use:** Refer to all, both, either, or neither of a set.

**Example:** Most of the tests passed. / Both options work. / Neither option works.

**Usage / decision:** Quantifier choice and agreement matter; do not collapse all versus both or either versus neither as synonyms. Connection notes own coordinated both...and/either...or/neither...nor, a different structure.

**Replacement disposition:** [N161](cards.md#allmostsome--noun--allmostsome-of--determinerpronoun), [N162](cards.md#both--plural-noun--eitherneither--singular-noun--botheitherneither-of--identified-pair)

**Sources:** ADD41.

<a id="add42"></a>

### ADD42 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `a/an + singular count noun / the + identified noun / plural or uncountable noun for a general class`

**Use:** Choose indefinite, identified, or general reference.

**Example:** I need a laptop. / The laptop you recommended is expensive. / Laptops are useful for travel.

**Usage / decision:** A compact production contrast can address recurring article errors. This is not a complete article system; context, institutional expressions, and proper names need separate targeted treatment.

**Replacement disposition:** [N174](cards.md#aan--singular-count-noun--the--identified-noun--pluraluncountable-noun-for-a-general-class)

**Sources:** ADD42.

<a id="add43"></a>

### ADD43 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `person who + clause / thing that/which + clause / place where + clause`

**Use:** Specify which person or thing you mean.

**Example:** I need someone who can review the code. / Is there a place where I can charge my laptop?

**Usage / decision:** Existing P102/P103 and the relative-clause resource already cover useful routes. Preserve full relative clauses; reduction and omission remain owned by their reviewed grammar cards.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P102](#existing-p102), [P103](#existing-p103).

**Replacement disposition:** [N213](cards.md#noun--whothatwhich--clause--noun--whose--noun--clause--placetime--wherewhen--clause)

**Sources:** ADD43.

<a id="add44"></a>

### ADD44 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `give/send + person + thing / give/send + thing + to + person; buy + thing + for + person`

**Use:** Describe giving or doing something for someone.

**Example:** I'll send you the file. / I'll send the file to you. / I bought a charger for her.

**Usage / decision:** Existing connection cards teach these alternations. Do not extend the two-object form to every verb: explain the issue to me, not explain me the issue.

**Original home / related coverage:** Connection evidence: [C02-117](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-117), [C02-118](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-118), [C02-119](../connections/02-verb-patterns.md#c02-119).

**Replacement disposition:** [N188](cards.md#verb--direct-object--verb--object--preposition-phrase--givesend--person--thing--thing--to--person)

**Sources:** ADD44.

<a id="add47"></a>

### ADD47 · VOCABULARY

**Pattern:** `Let's say / Suppose + clause`

**Use:** Offer a hypothetical example.

**Example:** Let's say the server goes down. What should happen next? / Suppose we have twice as many users.

**Usage / decision:** Already in vocabulary under that said / let’s say. Reuse that note; don't mistake let's say for a contrast marker.

**Original home / related coverage:** Vocabulary: `that said / let’s say` in [the current master](../vocabulary/data/vocabulary_master.csv).

**Replacement disposition:** [N078](cards.md#in-case--clause--just-in-case--in-case-of--noun), [N079](cards.md#what-if--clause--lets-saysuppose--clause)

**Sources:** ADD47.

<a id="add48"></a>

### ADD48 · REFERENCE

**Pattern:** `position → reason → example → qualification → conclusion`

**Use:** Maintain an argument across several sentences.

**Example:** I'd choose the smaller model. It responds faster. For example, it meets our target on older hardware. That said, we should test its accuracy first.

**Usage / decision:** A practice task using existing frames, not a new Front with an essay formula. Choose only the moves the situation needs.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P054](#existing-p054), [P005](#existing-p005), [P064](#existing-p064), [P067](#existing-p067).

**Replacement disposition:** [N040](cards.md#firstfirst-of-all--clause--thennextafter-that--clause--finally--clause), [N041](cards.md#the-main-point-is-that--clause--the-bottom-line-is-that--clause--at-the-end-of-the-day--clause), [N042](cards.md#to-sum-upin-summaryin-conclusionoverall--clause--long-story-short--clause)

**Sources:** ADD48.

<a id="add49"></a>

### ADD49 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `the + superlative + noun; one of the + superlative + plural noun; the + superlative + noun + I've ever + past participle`

**Use:** Identify the strongest example in a comparison set.

**Example:** This is the simplest solution. / It's one of the most useful tools I've tried.

**Usage / decision:** Use a plural noun after one of the. A superlative ranks within a set; it is not the same as saying better than one alternative. Keep best/worst as ordinary irregular forms.

**Replacement disposition:** [N147](cards.md#the--superlative--noun--one-of-the--superlative--plural-noun--the--superlative--noun--i-have-ever--past-participle)

**Sources:** ADD49.

<a id="add50"></a>

### ADD50 · EXPAND

**Pattern:** `instead of + noun or V-ing / rather than + parallel alternative`

**Use:** Choose an alternative action or thing.

**Example:** We took the train instead of driving. / I'd rather test it now than fix it after release.

**Usage / decision:** Instead of commonly takes a noun/V-ing; rather than permits several parallel forms, including base verbs. Existing preference and connection notes already cover much of the boundary; add the everyday instead-of option rather than duplicating all of them.

**Original home / related coverage:** Patterns: [P082](#existing-p082), [P083](#existing-p083); Connection evidence: [C08-035](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-035), [C08-036](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-036), [C08-037](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-037), [C08-038](../connections/08-coordination-parallelism.md#c08-038).

**Replacement disposition:** [N091](cards.md#prefer--nounv-ing--to--nounv-ing--would-prefer-to--verb--would-rather--base-verb--than--base-verb--instead-of--nounv-ing--rather-than--parallel-alternative)

**Sources:** ADD50.

<a id="add51"></a>

### ADD51 · CANDIDATE

**Pattern:** `as + adjective/adverb + as possible / as + adjective/adverb + as + subject + can`

**Use:** Ask for the greatest feasible degree within practical limits.

**Example:** Please reply as soon as you can. / Keep the explanation as clear as possible.

**Usage / decision:** This sets a practical degree limit. As soon as you arrive links two events and is a different time construction. Avoid treating every urgent request as ASAP.

**Replacement disposition:** [N146](cards.md#as--adjectiveadverb--as--not-as--adjectiveadverb--as--comparative--than-expected--as--adjectiveadverb--as-possibleyou-can)

**Sources:** ADD51.

## Existing Patterns card index

This index covers all 145 Fronts read from [cards.md](cards.md) on 2026-10-20. Use the exact Front to find the current Back. The P-numbers are local audit references, not stable Anki note IDs.

If a card is not named in a consolidated entry, **account for its useful coverage before retiring it**. Index entries saying “retain existing coverage” do not require preserving the same standalone note. A replacement may merge or rewrite it; an intentional omission needs a reason. In particular, the catalog originally omitted several valuable areas already taught: object complements, relative/circumlocution frames, emphatic addition, paired comparisons, numerical change, travel/service patterns, and the reviewed omission/shortening suite. Absence from the generated list alone is not grounds for deletion.

The index verifies presence, not a new completeness audit of every old Back. Omission-card content remains governed by [its full specification](OMISSION_GRAMMAR_CARD_SPEC.md); this review does not modify or compress that suite.

## Review and import boundary

The complete replacement is in [cards.md](cards.md), and the matching [Anki import](anki-import.tsv) is generated from it. Review that complete source rather than treating the brief catalog entries as card Backs.

1. Changes requested during review should update the nearest existing family rather than append a redundant synonym card.
2. A revised grouped card must retain the shared meaning, the useful structural differences, and an example for each displayed alternative.
3. After any card changes, update the production selection when necessary and run the documented pattern builder and validation.
4. This is a replacement source, not a migration performed inside desktop Anki. Import matching does not automatically delete old notes. Retire the old Patterns notes explicitly when adopting the approved replacement; do not delete the separate Vocabulary or Connection Patterns decks.
5. Use new contexts to check active production. A correct response to a familiar example does not by itself demonstrate flexible use in conversation.

No numerical coverage percentage is claimed. Future genuine gaps should improve the nearest existing family rather than grow an unlimited collection of synonyms.
